# HANDOFF_ARCHIVE.md — history of the Orchestrator's continuity log

**This file is history, not the current state.** The live state is `HANDOFF.md`. This archive is NOT read at the start of a chat; the Orchestrator searches it (`project_search`) or reads one part of it when a past decision, a gate result or an earlier session matters. Nothing here is edited afterwards except to append. Created 2026-11-12 (Adam's request) by moving out of `HANDOFF.md`, word for word: the whole decision log (185 entries, 2026-11-05 to 2026-11-12), the session entries older than the three newest, and the Current State rows as they stood at the mid-morning handoff of 2026-11-12.

**Gate numbers.** The roadmap was restructured on 2026-11-11 (decision log entry "ctxmon UI is its own milestone, M5"). Entries dated before that use the OLD numbers. Mapping: old M2.7, M2.8, M2.9, M2.10, M2.11 are now M5.1, M5.2, M5.3, M5.4, M5.6; old M3.6 is now M5.5; old M4.5 is now M5.7; old M4.6 became M4.5 (DOGFOOD 2) and M5.8 (the v0.1 tag); old M5.x, M6.x, M7.x and M8 are now M6.x, M7.x, M8.x and M9; DOGFOOD 3 is now M6.5. DOGFOOD 1 is now the light editor at M2.6b.

**Gate numbers and versions, 2026-11-17.** Entries dated before 2026-11-17 use the numbers and version labels of that morning. Mapping (Adam's decisions of that day): the agent milestone, old M6.x, is now M5.x and the ctxmon UI milestone, old M5.x, is now M6.x (the weights followed their content); the old M6.5 (diff engine) and old M6.11 (DiffView) are now one gate, M2.6, in the editor base, and their numbers are not reused; the old M2.6, M2.6b and M2.6c are now M2.7, M2.7b and M2.7c. Versions now follow the milestones: M1 to M3 are 0.1.0, M4 0.2.0, M5 (agent) 0.3.0, M6 (ctxmon) 0.4.0, M7 0.5.0, M8 0.6.0, M9 0.9.0, then the beta and 1.0.0; the old labels v0.1 (editor, ctxmon, build), v0.2 (agent), v0.3 (debugger) and v0.4 (cost, project file, gates) no longer exist.

**What is in the decision log.** Decisions (many now written in ARCHITECTURE.md, AGENTS.md, AI_ONBOARDING.md, the roadmap or `docs/ideas.md`, which are the live copies) mixed with the gate execution record (who ran what, audit verdicts, hashes, commits). Where an entry and a live doc disagree, the live doc wins.

# Part 1. Decision log (verbatim, oldest first)

- 2026-11-05 — (Adam) **Reset.** The project is rebuilt from scratch. The earlier code base is abandoned together with every decision tied to it (MIT licence, custom editing widget, its syntax format, regex engine and feature set, and its removal roadmap).
- 2026-11-05 — (Adam) **Scope trimmed** to: a directory sidebar on the right-hand side (specification kept in `docs/requirements/directory-sidebar.md`), an integrated agent (OpenAI standard, Anthropic and Gemini supported), the ctxmon model monitor ported from `~/Projects/llm-suite-test/ctxmon_gtk.py`, building code in the application and debugging it. Added: a terminal (VTE, also in the monitor), a project file, slash commands with a gate framework, MCP, cost display. Dropped from the first scope: git integration, multi-cursor, plugin hook, theme system, SSH, session restore, indexed project search.
- 2026-11-05 — (Adam) **Language C++17** (after weighing C): RAII and `std::string` for the logic-heavy modules; GTK 4 and GLib through their plain C APIs. **No C++ exceptions in project code** (GTK callbacks are C). **No wrapper rule**: nothing that exists only to wrap another API; libraries that provide a widget or service (GtkSourceView, VTE, libsoup, json-glib, libsecret) are fine.
- 2026-11-05 — (Adam) **Licence GPL-3.0-or-later**, copyright "Adam Hainstock (@adamjeremy-sk)", SPDX header on every source file. Supersedes MIT. The licence text is taken from the GPL-3.0 text in `llm-suite-test` (Adam's own repository, which holds ctxmon).
- 2026-11-05 — (Adam) **Editor widget: GtkSourceView 5.** Target system Ubuntu 24.04.5 (gdb 15.1; GTK 4.14 expected). Development packages to install: `libgtk-4-dev libgtksourceview-5-dev libjson-glib-dev libsoup-3.0-dev libsecret-1-dev libvte-2.91-gtk4-dev libgtest-dev cmake pkg-config clang-format clang-tidy build-essential`.
- 2026-11-05 — (Adam) **Agent**: own agent loop with provider adapters (OpenAI-compatible first, which also reaches Gemini's compatible endpoint; native Anthropic; native Gemini only if needed); streaming; direct model access with user keys in the keyring; tools run_command and the seven others. **Permission presets** Restricted (default), Balanced (Adam's preference, like the coding session), Unrestricted, and Custom JSON. Unrestricted needs a confirmation dialog and a persistent red badge; in every preset the agent cannot change its own preset and cannot read API keys. Setup dialog on first use of the Agent tab. `/clear` archives the chat; `/history`, `/copy`; Copy buttons everywhere; slash commands as Markdown templates, with the gate framework (`/gate`, `/audit`) as built-in ones. Cost display with user-defined input and output prices (including for a local model). Protocols: native loop and MCP (server and client over stdio); ACP deferred. Context, streaming, keys, review, undo, privacy and limits as in ARCHITECTURE.md section 7.6.
- 2026-11-05 — (Adam) **Review and external changes**: side-by-side diff (option B) with folding and per-hunk Accept/Revert; external changes reload a clean buffer automatically and show a banner for a dirty one.
- 2026-11-05 — (Adam) **Debugger**: Debug Adapter Protocol, gdb's built-in DAP first (gdb 15.1), GDB/MI as the fallback if the M6.2 spike shows DAP unreliable. **Build**: per-folder configuration in `.vermilion/project.json` with presets for CMake, Make, Ninja, Cargo, Gradle and Bazel, problem matchers for gcc, clang, rustc, javac.
- 2026-11-05 — (Adam) **Project file** (`PROJECT.md` plus `.vermilion/project.json`; names proposed) tracks the project layout and decisions as the user builds; created by New Project and by "Adopt as project".
- 2026-11-05 — (Adam) **Order and versions**: v0.1 editor, sidebar, build/Output/Problems, terminal; v0.2 agent core and diff; v0.3 debugger; v0.4 monitor panel, cost, Anthropic/Gemini, project file, gate framework, MCP. ctxmon logic (M1) is built early as the first job for Qwen, with tests derived from the Python original. Application ID `io.github.adamjeremy_sk.Vermilion`.

- 2026-11-08 — (Adam) **Gate M1.7 (parser hardening) before M2, all five topics decided with Adam.** Content: `vram` reads only ordinary files, at most 64 bytes, anything else is an unreadable card; `parse_slots` rejects a negative `n_ctx` or `n_decoded` where the answer is read; JSON bodies over 1 MiB are rejected in core, and a depth check before parsing (nesting over 32 levels, brackets inside strings ignored, escapes handled) rejects deeper text, because json-glib 1.8.0 on the target has no depth guard; the time-to-full estimate is removed (`eta`, its alert, `kEtaWarnSeconds`, `eta_warned_`, the oracle's `eta` field and the two scenarios `time_to_full_alert` and `time_to_full_after_down`); three hand tests lock in `live_speed` (equal timestamps, clock going backwards, count not growing; the code already returns no speed there); hostile-input hand tests for every parser (1 MB lines, deep nesting, huge numbers); the log parsers keep the 512 KiB tail and get a large-input test; the oracle is edited only to drop `eta` and the two scenarios.
- 2026-11-08 — (Adam) **M1.7 topic 1 decided: `vram` file reads.** Only ordinary files, at most 64 bytes; anything else (link to an endless source, pipe, oversized file) counts as an unreadable card. Recommended by the Orchestrator, confirmed by Adam.
- 2026-11-08 — (Adam) **M1.7 topic 2 decided: odd server numbers.** A negative `n_ctx` or `n_decoded` is rejected where the server answer is read (treated as not given), so the rest of the monitor sees only valid numbers. Recommended by the Orchestrator, confirmed by Adam; the difference from the Python gets a code comment and a hand test. (The Orchestrator's first proposal also said a live speed of zero or less counts as none; reading the code showed `live_speed` already does that, so there is nothing to add; see topic 5.)
- 2026-11-08 — (Adam) **M1.7 topic 3 decided: size limits.** A JSON answer over 1 MiB is rejected inside the core (treated as no answer); the log text keeps its 512 KiB tail window plus a test that very large input finishes quickly. Recommended by the Orchestrator, confirmed by Adam.
- 2026-11-08 — (Adam) **M1.7 topic 4 decided: JSON nesting depth.** Our own depth check before parsing: nesting over 32 levels is rejected (brackets inside quoted strings ignored, escapes handled); real answers nest 3 or 4 levels. Reason: the target has json-glib 1.8.0, and per Adam's notes (not verified by the Orchestrator, whose source lookup failed) the depth guard `JSON_PARSER_MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH` (1024, error `JSON_PARSER_ERROR_NESTING`) arrived only in json-glib 1.10, so 1.8 is unbounded. The 1 MiB cap stays: it bounds memory and time, the depth check bounds recursion. Checkable in the cloud workspace, since it uses no library.
- 2026-11-08 — (Adam) **M1.7 topic 5 decided: equal timestamps.** No code change: the Python and the C++ `live_speed` already return no speed when the newest reading is not later than the oldest in the window or the count did not grow (a clock going backwards, NaN times and falling counts end the same way). Three hand tests lock this in (equal timestamps, clock going backwards, count not growing).

- 2026-11-11 — (Adam, option A, Orchestrator recommendation) **Mixed line endings**: GtkSourceView 5.12 keeps each line's ending in the buffer, takes the file's type from the first line only, and its saver writes one ending on every line (Enter always inserts LF). The core counts the endings on load; a mixed file shows "Mixed" with a status message naming the ending the save will use, which is the one most lines have. Option B (writing each line's own ending ourselves) declined. (Orchestrator, Adam deferred) **BOM**: the loader never checks a byte-order mark and would accept almost any Windows-1252 file as UTF-16, so the core reads the first bytes itself: a UTF-16 mark gives only the matching UTF-16 candidate; otherwise UTF-8, Windows-1252, Latin-1; a UTF-8 mark is recorded and written back. Roadmap M2.2 row updated.
- 2026-11-11 — (Adam) **README checklist** in AI_ONBOARDING.md 6.2, after the banner still said 117 tests at 132; banner fixed.
- 2026-11-11 — (Adam, option 1 of 2, Orchestrator recommendation) **M2.2 reading and writing**: GtkSourceView's `GtkSourceFileLoader` and `GtkSourceFileSaver` do the work, steered by a new core scanner that reads the whole file once in blocks before loading and decides the encoding (whole-file UTF-8 check, since the loader checks only the first block; Windows-1252 unless one of its five undefined bytes appears, then Latin-1), the byte-order mark, the longest line, NUL bytes and the majority line ending; the loader then gets exactly one candidate encoding. The BOM stays in the text as an invisible U+FEFF and is written back (UTF-16 read as UTF-16LE/BE keeps it; checked with iconv). Saving goes through GLib's `g_file_replace`: temporary file beside the original, fsync, rename; a file with several hard links or a symbolic link is rewritten in place after the fsync (GLib 2.80 source). The saver's "changed on disk" refusal gets an Overwrite / Cancel question. "Zero bytes" in the roadmap means NUL bytes (binary); an empty file opens without a question. Option 2 (own conversion and writing code) declined. M2.2 runs in two Qwen sessions: steps 1 to 3 (scanner, `DocumentList` paths and formats, File → Open with status panels and Word Wrap), then steps 4 to 6 (Save, close and quit questions, Encoding and Line Ending menus, Adam's measurements), written after the first is audited.

- 2026-11-11 (night) — (Adam) **M2.2c talk held**, five topics one at a time, each with the Orchestrator's recommendation. (1, A) **Where**: recovery copies live in the XDG state folder, `$XDG_STATE_HOME/vermilion/recovery/<mode>/` (`~/.local/state/vermilion/recovery/`), a subfolder per mode (`full`, `feather`) so a program only looks at its own; the lookup is one core function with an injectable base so tests use a temporary directory (first code to write under XDG; settings at M2.5 reuse it). Beside the original (B) and the cache folder (C) declined. (2, B) **When**: 5 s after typing stops and at the latest 30 s after the first unsaved change since the last copy (constants until the M2.5 settings); only documents with unsaved edits, none for read-only or still-loading ones; one document at a time; the copy is deleted on save and on Close Without Saving (also at quit); a failed write (full disk) shows one status message and then stays quiet. (3, B with D, E parked) **Very large documents**: the copy times its own main-loop freeze and the next copy waits at least 20 times that; above a ceiling size there is no copy and the status bar says "Too large for recovery copies"; the first code step measures the freeze of `gtk_text_buffer_get_text` on a 39 MB buffer and the number goes to Adam before the ceiling is fixed; if the freeze is over about 100 ms the copy is taken in 1 MB slices on idle callbacks, discarded and restarted if the buffer changes meanwhile (D). Adam asked whether a file in RAM written to disk by a background process would avoid the lag: no, the freeze is the text extraction from GTK's segment tree on the main thread, the disk write is already asynchronous, and `/dev/shm` would not survive a power cut. The edit journal (E: append each edit to a log, replay it on the original) is parked in `docs/ideas.md`. (4, A) **Privacy**: plain files, folder 0700, files 0600, the agent's file tools barred from the folder (hard guard, M6), no automatic age-out (silently deleting unsaved work contradicts recovery; start-up asks until Adam decides); encryption (B) and exclusion of sensitive file names (C) declined. (5, A) **Start-up**: one question per document in turn, as quit does; buttons Recover, Discard, Decide Later (keeps the copy, asked again next start), "n of m" in the text and Recover All when two or more wait; it shows the time of the copy and the file's modified time; no Compare button until the diff view (M6.9); a recovered document opens modified with its original path (untitled if it had none; the existing "changed on disk" question applies at save); no blank start document is made when any copy is recovered; each copy is written under a temporary name and renamed, leftover temporary files are removed at start-up; one window listing all copies (B) declined as heavier UI. Adam: "Lean is what we're after." The file format counts as a config format, so it is planned with Adam first (AGENTS.md rule 10).

- 2026-11-12 — (Orchestrator, within the M2.2c design) **The recovery store does not use GIO.** Writing the store tests against the real GLib 2.80 showed that `g_file_replace` (`G_FILE_CREATE_PRIVATE | G_FILE_CREATE_REPLACE_DESTINATION`) uses a temporary file only when the destination already exists; for a new id it writes the destination itself and in place, so a failed write left a partial `.vrec` under a real name. The store therefore writes a temporary file in the same folder (`g_mkstemp_full`, 0600), flushes it with `fsync` and renames it (`g_rename`); no GIO, no new dependency, and ARCHITECTURE.md 7.11 now says so (leftovers are `.vrec-tmp-*`, not `.goutputstream-*`). Reads open with `O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK`, accept only regular files of at most 1 GiB (`kRecoveryMaxFileBytes`, checked before reading) and read only the first 32 KiB for a header check. **STEP 3 is two prompts**: 3a (header, util header, 19 tests, spec of the `.cpp`, about 30 KB) and 3b (15 tests); tests 311, 330, 345. Checked here before issuing: 34 tests under ASan and UBSan against the real GLib, g++ and clang 18 strict flags, clang-format clean, 45 of 48 mutants killed (survivors equivalent or untestable). The reference `.cpp` is 332 lines (over the 300 review line, one responsibility).

- 2026-11-12 — (Adam) **M2.2c STEP 4 design, three topics decided** (each with the Orchestrator's recommendation, taken). (1, option A) **Core class plus thin UI**: a display-free core class decides (one recovery id per document made once, which documents are eligible, the header from a document's record, the size ceiling, the schedule) and is tested without a display; a thin UI class `recovery_controller` does only the GTK plumbing (timer, text from the buffer, worker thread, status message, the logged hold); the large UI files get small hooks only. (2, option A) **`file-mtime` is read after load and after save** with an async GIO query and remembered per document (the main loop never waits); a recovered document is built from the copy, so GtkSourceView's own changed-on-disk check has no base time, and this recorded time is what STEP 5's start-up question must use to warn before an overwrite (to be remembered at STEP 5). (3, option A) **A copy exists only while the document has unsaved changes**: it is deleted when the modified flag turns off (save, or Undo back to the saved text) and on Close Without Saving.

### Current State row "STEP 4 plan" (as it stood before the 4e audit)

| STEP 4 plan | Method: the Orchestrator dictates headers and tests, compiles and mutation-tests them here, Qwen writes the `.cpp` from a specification; one step per prompt. **4a done**: `recovery_tracker.h` (`RecoveryTracker(list, ids)`: `note_edit(id, chars, now)`, `note_clean`, `note_closed`, `set_file_mtime`, `wake_at`, `begin_next(now, copied_us)`, `end_copy(now, freeze_ms, written)`, `copy_ids`, `recovery_id_of`, `copy_in_flight`; `RecoveryEffects{delete_id, announce_too_large, announce_failure}`, `RecoveryJob{document, recovery_id, header}`), fixture and 10 tests; 345 to 355. **4b done, PASS**: `tests/recovery_tracker_rules_test.cpp` (11 tests) and `recovery_tracker_events_test.cpp` (17); 355 to 383. **4c** (hooks, small): a `DocumentObserver` interface; `EditorPage` gets an `edited` callback (buffer "changed", not while loading, and also from `mark_modified()`) and accessors `text()`, `char_count()`, `file_path()`; `DocumentTabs` gets `set_observer` and calls edited, modified(id, bool), closed (before the erase), reloading (in `reopen`, treated like `note_clean`) and synced (after a load succeeds and after a save reports Saved; the file time comes from an async GIO query on the editor's file location, not the record path). **4d**: `recovery_controller` (thin GTK class) and its wiring in `MainWindow` (member `recovery_` declared after `tabs_`, `tabs_.set_observer(&recovery_)`, destructor sets the observer to nullptr). Controller: the timer is armed only while something is pending (from `wake_at()`), fires, checks, re-arms; one single-thread `GThreadPool` (FIFO) runs writes and deletes; the destructor frees the pool with wait, then deletes the files still listed by `copy_ids()` (covers quit with Close Without Saving); the hold of every copy is logged under `G_MESSAGES_DEBUG=vermilion`; the text is taken with `gtk_text_buffer_get_text`, `chars` from `gtk_text_buffer_get_char_count`; program `RecoveryProgram::Light` until M2.6b; status messages "Too large for recovery copies" (once) and one after a failed write (once per run of failures); `remove_leftovers()` once at start-up; the modified flag is checked only when a copy begins (the buffer's "changed" fires before "modified-changed"). **For STEP 5 (do not forget)**: a recovered document is built from the copy, so GtkSourceView's own changed-on-disk check has no base time; the start-up question must compare the recorded `file-mtime` (read after load and after save) to warn before an overwrite. Checks for 4c and 4d as in M2.2b: stand-in GTK declarations, `nm -D` on every GTK symbol; GTK itself is not in the cloud workspace, GLib 2.80 with GIO is. |

# Part 6. Moved at the handoff of 2026-11-12 (night, after issuing 5e), verbatim

## Decision entries (oldest first)
- 2026-11-12 — (Orchestrator, within the M2.2c design) **The recovery store does not use GIO.** Writing the store tests against the real GLib 2.80 showed that `g_file_replace` (`G_FILE_CREATE_PRIVATE | G_FILE_CREATE_REPLACE_DESTINATION`) uses a temporary file only when the destination already exists; for a new id it writes the destination itself and in place, so a failed write left a partial `.vrec` under a real name. The store therefore writes a temporary file in the same folder (`g_mkstemp_full`, 0600), flushes it with `fsync` and renames it (`g_rename`); no GIO, no new dependency, and ARCHITECTURE.md 7.11 now says so (leftovers are `.vrec-tmp-*`, not `.goutputstream-*`). Reads open with `O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK`, accept only regular files of at most 1 GiB (`kRecoveryMaxFileBytes`, checked before reading) and read only the first 32 KiB for a header check. **STEP 3 is two prompts**: 3a (header, util header, 19 tests, spec of the `.cpp`, about 30 KB) and 3b (15 tests); tests 311, 330, 345. Checked here before issuing: 34 tests under ASan and UBSan against the real GLib, g++ and clang 18 strict flags, clang-format clean, 45 of 48 mutants killed (survivors equivalent or untestable). The reference `.cpp` is 332 lines (over the 300 review line, one responsibility).
- 2026-11-12 — (Adam) **M2.2c STEP 4 design, three topics decided** (each with the Orchestrator's recommendation, taken). (1, option A) **Core class plus thin UI**: a display-free core class decides (one recovery id per document made once, which documents are eligible, the header from a document's record, the size ceiling, the schedule) and is tested without a display; a thin UI class `recovery_controller` does only the GTK plumbing (timer, text from the buffer, worker thread, status message, the logged hold); the large UI files get small hooks only. (2, option A) **`file-mtime` is read after load and after save** with an async GIO query and remembered per document (the main loop never waits); a recovered document is built from the copy, so GtkSourceView's own changed-on-disk check has no base time, and this recorded time is what STEP 5's start-up question must use to warn before an overwrite (to be remembered at STEP 5). (3, option A) **A copy exists only while the document has unsaved changes**: it is deleted when the modified flag turns off (save, or Undo back to the saved text) and on Close Without Saving.
- 2026-11-12 — (Adam) **Decisions of STEP 4d and 4e.** Option B: a repo test for the controller (4d-3). Option 1: the first copy written after an announced failure replaces the failure message with "Recovery copies are working again." once (4e). The hands-on check of the wired program passed all eleven items (copy after 5 s of quiet, one file per document replaced in place, the 30 s cap, Save, Undo back to the saved text, uri and file time in the header, close tab and Reopen as, clean quit empties the folder, `kill -9` leaves the copy, a failed write announced once with a warning every 5 s and recovery after `chmod 700`, a leftover `.vrec-tmp-*` removed at start); the main loop was held 0 ms per copy.
- 2026-11-12 — (Orchestrator) **STEP 5a audited PASS.** The three dictated files have the given sha256 (checked on disk; `recovery_tracker.h` is byte-identical to the reference, `recovery_offer.cpp` f6cedbc8… and `recovery_tracker.cpp` b5d1c176… differ from the reference only in layout and variable names). Qwen's `.cpp` files were run here with the 16 new tests and the 38 tracker tests (54 of 54, ASan and UBSan) and are clang-format clean; Qwen's build: 400/400, 0 warnings. No prompt doubt (Qwen noted that `git diff` cannot show edits to untracked files; known). Mirror note: this file's Project copy is current as of this entry.
- 2026-11-12 — (Orchestrator) **STEP 5b audited PASS.** The two dictated files have the given sha256 (4cd3a086…, 11168f26…; checked on disk); Qwen's `recovery_question.cpp` (abc83627…) differs from the reference (eaa921af…) in layout and structure only; run here with the 14 tests (ASan, UBSan, g++), clang-format clean, clang syntax check clean; Qwen's build: 414/414, 0 warnings. ## Part 7 — rotated out of HANDOFF.md at the handoff after the 5f audit (2026-11-12)

### Decision entries (oldest first)
- 2026-11-12 — (Orchestrator, within the STEP 5 design) **STEP 5c designed and issued.** Facts that bind 5d: (1) `DocumentTabs::add_recovered(Recovered)` (path optional, format, mixed flag, `file_mtime_us`, text) returns the new `DocumentId`, makes the tab active, tells the observer nothing and does not adopt the copy; a path that is already open (or no path) gives an untitled document inside the function; the insert is one `gtk_text_buffer_set_text` outside the undo history and is logged under `G_MESSAGES_DEBUG=vermilion` ("recovery: N characters inserted in M ms"); the caller must have checked the text (UTF-8, no NUL, at most `kRecoveryMaxFileBytes`). (2) The first-save check lives in `EditorPage::save`: `expect_file_time(path, mtime)` keeps a `RecoveredBase`; the first save to that path reads the file's time (`query_time`, async) and, when it differs, ends with `DocumentSaver::Outcome::ChangedOnDisk` and writes nothing, so `SaveController` asks Save Anyway as it already does; a gone file is not a change; Save Anyway, Save As to another path, a successful save and `load()` all drop the check. The two decisions are the pure functions `needs_base_check` and `file_changed_since_base` in `src/core/recovered_base.*` (8 tests). (3) `clearing_` is reused so the restore insert is not an edit for the observer; the recovered document therefore has no new copy until the next real edit, and the controller must adopt the old copy's id in 5d. (4) `EditorPage::saving()` is also true while the file's time is read. (5) Checked here before issuing: the core pair with 10 mutants; the four UI files stable under clang-format and compiled against stand-in GTK declarations; `EditorPage` run on real GLib with fake widgets (all cases, ASan and UBSan, 10 mutants killed). `editor_page.cpp` is 316 lines and `document_tabs.cpp` 443 after 5c; look at splitting `document_tabs.cpp` at the gate's end.
- 2026-11-12 — (Orchestrator) **STEP 5d audited PASS; the plan is now seven prompts (5a to 5g).** The seven E1 sha256 match on disk (`recovery_answers.h` d9940745…, `recovery_answers.cpp` 48793683…, tests 1991236c… and 2ccaae52…, controller 27483ebc… and acf1fe1c…, CMake 2e7c00d7… and d3733a1e…); Qwen's `recovery_answers.cpp` (48793683…) differs from my reference (defb7e13…) in layout only; Qwen's build: 444/444, 0 warnings, fresh evidence (`s5d_*`). **The old 5e is cut in three**, because the dialog flow can be tested here only if it is split from the GTK: **5e** `src/ui/recovery_io.*` (`query_file_time`: GIO async, callback on the main loop; `read_recovery_copy`: `GTask` worker thread, `RecoveryStore::read` then `recovery_text_problem`; returns `CopyRead{copy, problem}`) and `src/core/recovery_blank.*` (`is_untouched_blank(record, chars)`), 5 + 11 tests, new test exe `vermilion_recovery_io_tests` linking `PkgConfig::GTK4` for the GIO headers; **5f** the `RecoveryPrompt` class with a fake `ask` in its tests; **5g** the `MainWindow` and `main.cpp` wiring. Numbering decision: the blank start document is closed immediately before the first recovered document is added, so an untitled recovery reuses "Untitled 1" (`DocumentList::add_untitled` takes the lowest free N). 5e checked here before issuing: both test files under ASan and UBSan (the io tests also with clang 18) against real GLib/GIO 2.80 and a real main loop; 18 mutants, all killed except one that does not build; the prompt parsed back from its Markdown, the four dictated files byte-identical.

### M2.2c active-gate decisions as they stood at the docs sweep (2026-11-12; ARCHITECTURE.md 7.11 and the roadmap hold the result)
- 2026-11-12 — (Orchestrator) **M2.2c STEP 4 (the wiring) is complete: 4a to 4e, every step audited PASS.** The audit records (hashes, checks, mutants) and the superseded STEP 4 plan are in the archive, Part 4; reference implementations and mutants are in `claude/M2.2c_4_reference.md`; the prompts are the Project docs `claude/M2.2c_4a_tracker.md`, `_4b_files.md`, `_4c_hooks.md`, `_4d1_controller.md`, `_4d2_wiring.md`, `_4d3_test.md`, `_4e_working_again.md`. STEP 4 was cut into five prompts by the gate-size rule (one step, about 25 KB). **What binds STEP 5 and later:** (1) the recovery ids are random UUIDs, so a new run never overwrites the copies of a crashed one; they pile up in `~/.local/state/vermilion/recovery/light/` until STEP 5 reads them; (2) a permanently failing folder retries every 5 s with a warning log (the schedule's rule); (3) the controller (`src/ui/recovery_controller.*`) is GLib-only plumbing: the text and the status message are callbacks, the one-thread `GThreadPool` writes and deletes, the destructor deletes the files of `copy_ids()`; (4) `MainWindow` owns `recovery_` (declared after `formats_`, so it is destroyed first), `tabs_.set_observer(&recovery_)` before `add_untitled()`, and `~MainWindow()` sets the observer to nullptr; (5) the observer has six events, `document_saved` is an extra event so the copy is deleted whatever the order of the saver's modified-flag clearing, and the file time comes from `EditorPage::query_file_time()` (microseconds, guarded by `AsyncGuard`); (6) the controller test is ONE ctest entry (`RecoveryController.Timeline`, 15 checks over one shared timeline of about eleven seconds on the real clock, timeout 120 s), because `gtest_discover_tests` would run each `TEST_F` as its own process and repeat the timeline.
- 2026-11-12 — (Adam, Orchestrator) **M2.2c STEP 5 design decided (three topics, Adam; the rest within ARCHITECTURE.md 7.11).** (1, option A) A recovered document **takes over the old copy's id** (the tracker gets an `adopt_copy`), so its next copy replaces that file in place and the text is never without a copy; Save or Close Without Saving deletes it as usual. (2, option B) The question is an alert dialog like the close questions: text with the name, "n of m", the time of the copy, the file's time now and the changed-on-disk warning; buttons Recover, Discard, Recover All (when two or more wait) and Decide Later (Escape and default); **Discard asks "Delete this copy? This cannot be undone." first.** (3, option A) A copy whose file is already open (two crashed runs left two copies of one file) **opens as an untitled document**, and the status bar names the file. Orchestrator, no crossroads: the body is read and checked as UTF-8 on a worker thread (invalid UTF-8 or a NUL makes the copy damaged: Discard or Decide Later only) and inserted in one call on the main loop, timed in the log (slicing only if STEP 6 shows a need, as decided for 7.11); the first save of a recovered file document compares the file's current time with the recorded `file-mtime` and asks Save Anyway when it differs (GtkSourceView has no base time for it); the untouched blank start document is closed after the first recovery; Recover All leaves damaged copies where they are; untitled copies get the next free "Untitled N". **Plan (four prompts, revised 2026-11-12): 5a** core: `RecoveryTracker::adopt_copy` and `recovery_offer.*` (scan, order, "n of m", Decide Later, Recover All) with 16 tests; **5b** core: `recovery_question.*` (the path of a copy, the file-state verdict from the recorded `file-mtime`, the time format, the question texts); **5c** the document hooks (`DocumentTabs::add_recovered`, the first-save check against `file-mtime`); **5d** the prompt UI (worker-thread read, dialogs, Discard confirmation) and the `MainWindow` and `main.cpp` wiring, with the controller's `adopt` call.
- 2026-11-12 — (Adam, Orchestrator) **The `Gtk-WARNING ... Trying to snapshot GtkGizmo ... without a current allocation` is PARKED.** It was seen twice in Adam's hands-on run, at the first keystroke in a document (13:13:08 and 13:14:33, same widget address, once around a tab close and reopen). With recovery off (`XDG_STATE_HOME=/dev/null/x build/src/ui/vermilion`: the controller inactive, the 4c hooks on) it did not appear; under the CLion debugger with `G_DEBUG=fatal-warnings` and recovery on, Adam could not reproduce it with every function of the app. The controller makes no widget call, so it is probably an intermittent GTK or GtkSourceView warning that depends on timing. Not blocking. If it shows again Adam notes what he was doing and keeps the terminal output; look at it again at STEP 6.
- 2026-11-12 — (Orchestrator) **STEP 5e audited PASS; 5f designed and issued.** 5e: the six E1 sha256 match on disk; `recovery_blank.cpp` is byte-identical to my reference (`9e941e30…`); Qwen's `recovery_io.cpp` (`b44ba707…`) equals my reference (`df4a3c45…`) in behaviour (layout and names only); its first build failed in its own file (wrong GTask signatures), fixed without touching dictated text; 460/460, 0 warnings, fresh evidence (`s5e_*`). **5f design refinement:** `RecoveryPrompt` (`src/ui/recovery_prompt.*`) includes no GTK at all; the plan's "GTK only through `ui::ask`" became an injected `Host::ask` (5g binds `ui::ask` to the window), so the whole walk is tested here with a fake window, the real store and `recovery_io` and a real main loop (22 tests, ASan and UBSan, g++ 13 and clang 18; 23 mutants, 22 killed, one does not build). `Host{ask, close_blank, add_document, adopt, message}`; the prompt is a pimpl with `std::shared_ptr<Core>` and a `weak_from_this()` token in every late callback, so a question, read or file time that ends after the prompt is destroyed does nothing. Facts that bind 5g: (1) the call order for one recovered document is close_blank (first document only), add_document, adopt, then the "recovered as untitled" message when the copy had a path and the window did not keep it; (2) a refused `adopt` counts the copy as kept; (3) Recover All asks nothing, recovers the valid copies in order, leaves damaged and unreadable ones on disk and counts them in the closing message; (4) a copy whose text turns out bad after Recover is shown again as damaged (Discard or Decide Later); (5) the last button of every question is Cancel (Decide Later, or Cancel on the delete question) and any answer outside the list counts as it; (6) `start()` scans the folder on the main loop (headers only) and asks for a file time through `query_file_time` before each file copy's question; (7) `finished()` tells the wiring when the walk has ended.
- 2026-11-12 — (Orchestrator) **STEP 5f audited PASS after fix 1; 5g designed and issued.** Qwen's first 5f build passed, but 8 PromptTest cases failed on Adam's machine (22 of 22 here): `read_recovery_copy(store, item.id, [weak, item = std::move(item)](...))` evaluates `item.id` and the init-capture in an unspecified order; on Adam's GCC the lambda is built first, so the id was empty ("no folder or invalid id"). My reference captured `item` by copy, which is why it passed here. Fix 1 (same session, prompt `CONTINUATION`): `std::string id = item.id;` before the call in `recover()` and `recover_next()`; 482 of 482, 0 warnings, fresh evidence (`s5f_*`), the three E1 sha256 match, `recovery_prompt.cpp` 55fa80ce…. **Lesson for later prompts:** never read a member of an object in the same call that moves the object into a lambda capture. Qwen also kept investigating after the first failure instead of stopping and reporting, as the prompt said; the fix went to it as a same-session continuation. **5g facts:** (1) `MainWindow::offer_recovery()` (new file `main_window_recovery.cpp`, so `main_window.cpp` stays under 300 lines) builds the Host and starts the prompt; `main.cpp` calls it after `present()`, on first activation only; (2) `blank_id_` is recorded right after the start `tabs_.add_untitled()`, and `close_blank_document` closes it only while `is_untouched_blank` holds; (3) `prompt_` is declared after `recovery_`, so it is destroyed first; (4) the prompt gets its own `RecoveryStore(default_recovery_root(), RecoveryProgram::Light)`, the controller keeps its own; (5) checked here before issuing: the new file compiles under clang 18 with `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic` against stand-in GTK declarations, clang-format 18 leaves all four files unchanged, and the prompt was parsed back (the new file and the eight edits are byte-identical to the checked ones). There is no automatic test for the wiring; Adam's hands-on run is the check.

### Decision entry (the M6 re-plan; the roadmap, ARCHITECTURE.md 7.6 and `docs/ideas.md` hold the result)
- 2026-11-13 — (Adam, Orchestrator) **M6 re-planned: local first.** Adam's scope: the agent works with a local llama.cpp server through the OpenAI-compatible API; other OpenAI-compatible servers are a happy coincidence, not a goal. **Parked in `docs/ideas.md`** (nine rows): the Anthropic and Gemini adapters, the MCP server and client (the built-in agent calls its in-process registry directly; MCP would only serve outside agents), key storage in the keyring (keys are `env:NAME` references; the key resolver answers `keyring:` with "not supported in this version"; the editor removes every variable named as a key reference from the agent shell's environment), context summarising (the loop stops with "context full, use /clear" instead), histogram diff, the heavy parts of change sets (persisted change sets, per-hunk Accept and Revert, one undo step per turn) and the `.rpm` packages with the Fedora VM. **Kept by Adam:** the permission engine with the Custom preset, the archive and `/history` as two gates, the diff engine (Myers, aligned rows, word-level, algorithm swappable), the full request log, cost display, the project file, slash commands and the gate framework. **Orchestrator's calls (Adam deferred them):** the indicator has three states, local (loopback), network (a private IP) and remote (anything else; a hostname other than `localhost` counts as remote, because resolving it would block the main loop); the gate order (permission engine before the tools, diff engine and lean change sets before the chat UI, DiffView late); DOGFOOD 4 confirmed at the chat UI. **New numbering** (old to new is in the roadmap, M6 Status line): M6 has 12 gates (M6.1 provider, M6.2 permission engine, M6.3 tools, M6.4 loop, M6.5 diff engine, M6.6 lean change sets, M6.7 chat UI, M6.8 setup dialog, M6.9 archive, `/clear`, `/copy`, M6.10 `/history`, M6.11 DiffView, M6.12 request log and indicator); M8 has three (M8.1 cost, M8.2 project file, M8.3 slash commands and gate framework). **Reweighed** (Adam asked the Orchestrator to): M0 6, M1 7, M2 15, M3 7, M4 10, M5 8, M6 23, M7 11, M8 6, M9 7; progress 21%. The unused `libsecret` requirement stays in `CMakeLists.txt`, the CI install line and the README package list until M6.1 removes it. **Docs changed:** roadmap, ARCHITECTURE.md (2, 4, 6, 7.6, 7.11, 7.12, 8, 10), AI_ONBOARDING.md (rail 7, section 4), AGENTS.md (the key rule), README, `docs/ideas.md`. Where the M2.2c entries went: the archive, Parts 7 and 8.

### Decision text of 2026-11-16 night as first written (moved at the handoff after STEP 13c)

- 2026-11-16 (night) — (Orchestrator, design; Adam can veto) **STEP 13 became five prompts, 13a to 13e, and `ReplaceController` got `start_replace_all()`.** The first plan of 13b (the job with its prerequisites) was a 50 KB prompt, so it was split: 13b the prerequisites (closed PASS), 13c the job (closed PASS WITH NOTES), 13d the window wiring, 13e the deletion. The old synchronous `replace_all()` stays, guarded (an empty result while a job runs), until 13e, so its existing tests keep proving the library loop and the build stays green. `SearchController` gained `set_on_buffer_changing` (called in `set_buffer()` before the old session is destroyed, so a running job ends first and keeps what it replaced) and `set_status_frozen` (the match count changes with every edit, so the status line is not recomputed while a job runs and refreshes once at the end). The order in `finish_job` matters: stop the timer, end the run, put the cursor back (whole-document job), log, reset the job, show the message, unfreeze, call the callback; so `status()` and `job_active()` are already right inside the callbacks. 13c's prompt was 47.7 KB (over the 40 KB guideline; the dictated tests are 18.6 KB); Qwen finished it in one run, and all 17 job tests, including the cursor mark and "undo is one step" ones that could not run in the cloud (no GtkSourceView), passed on Adam's machine.

### Active-gate decisions (M2.3 and later; M2.2c's moved to the archive, Parts 7 and 8; the M6 re-plan of 2026-11-13, held by the roadmap, ARCHITECTURE.md 7.6 and `docs/ideas.md`, in Part 9; the M6 toolset talk of 2026-11-14 and the README trim in Part 12; the session entry of the M6 toolset evening in Part 13; the prompt history of S7 in Part 15)

- 2026-11-13 — (Adam) **M2.3 design talk, topic 1: who does the searching: Option C, the hybrid.** The engine is `GRegex` in every case; RE2 and Oniguruma are not adopted (a new dependency needs Adam's decision, and only if runaway patterns prove a real problem). GtkSourceView's search context (`GtkSourceSearchContext`) does Find Next and Previous, highlighting, the match count and Replace; core holds what is testable without a display: the options model (text, case, whole word, regex on or off), regex validation with readable errors, the replacement-rule checks (no search history, topic 6). **The tests are our own** (Adam: CrimsonEditor's edge cases are not a source; the clone is for reading only). The first step of the gate reads the real 5.12 headers (read-only) and reports what is not yet confirmed (matching across line breaks, whether Replace All is one undo step, how its whole-word rule differs from letters, digits and underscore). Replace All is measured on the 50 MB test file by the gate's own code once Replace exists, logged under `G_MESSAGES_DEBUG=vermilion` (no throwaway program, Adam 2026-11-11), because the documentation says `replace_all` is synchronous and can block the UI; if it freezes the window, a follow-up gate gives Replace All sliced code of our own. The roadmap wording "core tests for the matcher" becomes "core tests for the search options and the replacement rules" in the docs sweep. Topics 2 onward follow in this talk.
- 2026-11-13 — (Adam) **M2.3 design talk, topic 2 (final): Find and Replace is a separate window (a dialog), not a bar.** Adam first chose a bar at the top (and, for the bar, one bar for the whole window above the tab strip), then changed his mind to a dialog the same evening; the bar decisions are void. **Every action must be reachable from the keyboard** (a hard requirement, to be fixed key by key in the talk). Because it is a window of its own, access keys (Alt plus a letter) on its controls do not clash with the menu bar's mnemonics. `GtkNotebook` and `main_window.cpp`'s layout are untouched. Whether Find and Replace are one dialog or two, and the rest of its behaviour, are the next topics.
- 2026-11-13 — (Adam) **M2.3 design talk, topic 3: two dialogs, as in Windows 2000.** A compact **Find** dialog (`Ctrl+F`) and a larger **Replace** dialog (`Ctrl+H`), both non-modal. Adam first asked for F8 (Find) and Alt+F8 (Replace) and then dropped it: the shortcuts stay `Ctrl+F`, `Ctrl+H`, `F3`, `Shift+F3`, `Ctrl+G` as in ARCHITECTURE.md 7.10. (Noted for later: on XFCE the window manager may take Alt+F8; F10 is GTK's menu-bar key and M7 gives it to Step Over; check both when they matter.)
- 2026-11-13 — (Adam) **M2.3 design talk, topic 4: the Find dialog.** Accepted as proposed: "Find what" entry (no history, see topic 6); checkboxes Match case, Match whole word only, Regular expression; a status line ("3 of 17 matches", "Not found", "Wrapped to the top", or the regex error in plain words); buttons Find Next (default), Find Previous, Close. Keys: `Ctrl+F` opens with the cursor in the entry (a one-line selection becomes the search text, selected; an open dialog comes forward), `Enter` Find Next, `Shift+Enter` Find Previous, `Esc` closes and returns to the text with the current match still selected, `F3` and `Shift+F3` work with the dialog open or closed, an `Alt` access key on every checkbox and button, `Tab` order in reading order, `Ctrl+C`, `X`, `V`, `A` act on the entry while it has focus (the M2.1 backlog item). **Find Previous and Find Next buttons, not Up/Down radio buttons. Wrap-around is always on** (status line says "Wrapped to the top"), no checkbox. **Matches highlight as you type with a debounce of 200 ms** (Adam: a constant, not every letter; each keystroke restarts the timer; `Enter` applies the pending text at once so a jump never uses a stale search); the caret moves only on `Enter` or Find Next.
- 2026-11-13 — (Adam) **M2.3 design talk, topic 5: the Replace dialog.** Same rows as Find plus "Replace with", a scope choice "Whole document" or "Selection only" (greyed out with no selection, preselected when the dialog opens with a multi-line selection), buttons Find Next (default), Replace, Replace All, Close; the status line adds "Replaced 42 matches". Keys: `Ctrl+H` opens with the cursor in "Find what" (in "Replace with" if "Find what" already has text); `Enter` in "Find what" is Find Next; `Enter` in "Replace with" is Replace; access keys Match **c**ase, **W**hole word, Regular e**x**pression, **R**eplace, Replace **A**ll, Find **N**ext; `Esc` closes. **Replace All is one undo step.** Adam said yes to all three points: (1) **Replace**: if the current selection is a match, it replaces it and selects the next match; otherwise it only selects the next match. (2) **"Selection only" is included** (GtkSourceView's `replace_all` has no range as far as known, so it means our own loop over the matches inside the selection; gate step 0 checks the API, and the item may be dropped if it turns out costly). (3) **Replacement text**: literal when Regular expression is off; with it on, GLib's replacement syntax (`\1` to `\9`, `\0` the whole match, `\t`, `\n`; the exact list is confirmed at gate step 0), no `&`; an invalid replacement shows an error in the status line.
- 2026-11-13 — (Adam) **M2.3 design talk, topic 6: Go to Line, the Search menu, history. The design talk is complete.** **Go to Line (`Ctrl+G`)**: a small window with one box; a line number or `line:column` (for example `120:8`); `Enter` jumps, `Esc` cancels; a number past the end jumps to the last line; the window shows the allowed range (for example "1 to 12,408"). **The Search menu**: Find, Find Next, Find Previous, Replace, Go to Line (as ARCHITECTURE.md 7.10); `F3` before anything has been searched opens Find. **No search history at all** (Adam, after hearing that memory-only was the alternative): no list, no dropdown, no Up and Down stepping, no saving; each dialog keeps its current text while the program runs, so `F3` and reopening work. Orchestrator's call, not discussed: the dialogs act on the active document and follow tab changes. **Still to do at the gate's docs sweep**: roadmap row M2.3 wording ("core tests for the search options and the replacement rules"), ARCHITECTURE.md 7.1 and 7.10 (two dialogs, no bar), a manual topic for search, and removal of these topic entries from this file.
- 2026-11-14 — (Adam) **M2.3 build-time decisions.** With Regular expression off, the search text AND the replacement are literal (a typed backslash is a backslash; newlines and tabs are searched with Regular expression on); no "extended" checkbox. STEP 4 showed the library already inserts a plain-mode replacement exactly as typed, so nothing is escaped on our side. The windows refuse a regex replacement GLib would accept but that would silently do the wrong thing: a group the pattern does not have, and a backslash followed by two or more digits (GLib reads it as a group number or as octal): the core says "use \g<12> for a group, or \x41 for a character".
- 2026-11-14 — (Orchestrator, from the probes; Adam can veto) **Library facts that shape the design** (full list in `claude/M2.3_reference_2.md`): Replace All is one undo step and always whole-buffer (so "Selection only" is our own loop, one undo step, skipping empty matches; Adam may still drop that item); with Regular expression and Match whole word both on the library mis-groups an alternation (`foo|bar` matched inside "foobar"), so the window hands it `(?:` pattern `)` (`effective_search_text`, STEP 5) and `pattern_error` checks that text too; empty-matching patterns (`^`, `x*`, `$`) match nothing ("Not found", "Nothing to replace"); an invalid pattern gives `replace_all` 0 with no error, so the window checks the pattern itself and shows the core's GLib-worded message (the library's own error text is never shown); "Wrapped to the top" only when a match was found and the search wrapped; Find Next searches from the selection end, Find Previous from its start; the "Searching" state is tracked by our code.
- 2026-11-15 — (Adam) **M2.3: the Find and Replace windows share ONE search.** The same "Find what" text and the same three checkboxes (Match case, Match whole word only, Regular expression) serve Ctrl+F and Ctrl+H; F3 and Shift+F3 act on that one search; the document is highlighted once; closing and reopening either window keeps the text. One `SearchController` (S7b) serves both windows, so a typed word is already in "Find what" when Ctrl+H opens. The replacement text and the scope (Whole document or Selection only) belong to the Replace window (S8). This refines topic 6 ("each dialog keeps its current text"): they keep the shared text.
- 2026-11-15 — (Orchestrator, process; Adam can veto) **STEP 7 is split in three, because the first plan was a 39 KB prompt:** S7a the `DebounceTimer` interface and `GlibTimer` (12 KB), S7b the display-free `SearchController` with 20 tests (32 KB), S7c the Find window and its wiring into `MainWindow`. Design of the controller: it owns the timer (injected, so tests use a fake), one `SearchSession` for the active buffer (a new session on a tab change; the old one is destroyed first so its highlighting goes), the typed options (what the window holds) and the applied options (what the session searches); typed text waits 200 ms (`kDebounceMs`), Enter or a checkbox applies at once, and applying options identical to the applied ones does nothing (a restart would lose the count); the status line is derived (message, then problem text, then match count and position) and its callback runs only on a change; a wrapped Find shows "Wrapped to the top" or "Wrapped to the bottom" until the next find or option change.
- 2026-11-15 — (Orchestrator, process and design; Adam can veto) **STEP 7c is split in two, and the Find window is built as follows.** 7c.1 = the `FindWindow` class (dictated header, Qwen writes the `.cpp`, GTK names checked against Adam's headers inside the step); 7c.2 = the wiring into `MainWindow`. The window is a plain `GtkWindow` (`gtk_window_new`), transient for the main window, not a `GtkApplicationWindow`, so the application's `win.` accelerators do not reach it and the entry keeps GTK's own Ctrl+C, X, V, A (the M2.1 backlog item; Adam tests it by hand after 7c.2). Access keys: _Find what, Match _case, Match _whole word only, Regular e_xpression, Find _Next (the default button), Find _Previous, Cl_ose (N and W as in topic 5; the Replace window of S8 keeps those keys and finds its own for the rest). Enter in the entry = Find Next (activates the default button), Shift+Enter = Find Previous (a capture-phase key controller on the entry), Esc = close (a capture-phase controller on the window); closing hides the window (never destroys it), so the text and checkboxes survive, and the main window then takes the focus back to the text. Match highlighting stays on after Close (F3 keeps working with the window closed); Adam judges that in the hands-on test. The status label's position ("3 of 17") is refreshed when the window becomes active, not on every cursor move (cheap; to be seen by Adam). **7c.2 also adds the Search menu with Find only** (Ctrl+F), so the window can be tried by hand at once; S10 adds Find Next, Find Previous, Replace, Go to Line and F3 (the rule "no dead menu items" holds).
- 2026-11-15 — (Adam) **Find window hands-on test: 24 of 24 PASS.** Ctrl+C, X, V, A work in the entry (the M2.1 backlog item for the Find window is closed; the Replace and Go to Line windows have to be checked the same way). **Highlights stay on after Close**: Adam did not like it at first and, after using it, likes it, so it is the design (F3 keeps working with the window closed). The "3 of 17" position refreshed on activation is accepted. With the last tab closed the Find window stays open, its buttons do nothing and do not crash, and Search > Find is greyed out; Adam passed that, so it is left as it is (S10 may decide whether the window should close with the last document).
- 2026-11-16 — (Adam) **M2.3: one window at a time.** When the Find window is open and Ctrl+H is pressed (or the Replace window is open and Ctrl+F is pressed), the open window hides and the other appears; the search text and the checkboxes carry over (they live in the shared `SearchController`). Asked as a plain question with the alternative (both windows open side by side, which would need the two to be kept in sync); Adam chose one at a time.
- 2026-11-16 — (Orchestrator, design; Adam can veto) **STEP 8 is split in three, and the Replace controller is built as follows.** 8a `ReplaceController` (display-free, `src/ui/replace_controller.{h,cpp}`, 25 tests), 8b the `ReplaceWindow` class, 8c the wiring. The scope (Whole document or Selection only) belongs to Replace All only; Find Next and Replace always work on the whole document. "Selection only" works on a range kept as two `GtkTextMark`s (start left gravity, end right gravity), so it follows the edits that Replace makes inside it and survives Find Next moving the selection; the range is taken from the document's selection when the window opens (`capture_selection`: a multi-line selection preselects Selection only, a one-line selection is the search text and leaves the scope on Whole document) and when the user picks Selection only (`choose_scope`: the current selection if there is one, otherwise a still usable remembered range, otherwise the choice is refused); another buffer drops the range, and `scope()` then reports Whole document. Messages: after the last match is replaced "Replaced 1 match", after a wrapped Replace "Wrapped to the top", after Replace All "Replaced N matches" with " in the selection" when the range was used (undo is one step). A bad replacement text shows while it is typed (status line, bold as a problem), after a bad pattern's own message. `SearchController` gains `buffer()`. `SearchController` has one status callback slot, so each window registers it in `open()` and clears it when it hides (the Find window is changed for this in 8c).
- 2026-11-16 — (Orchestrator, design; Adam can veto) **STEP 8b, the Replace window.** Layout: Find what / Replace with entries, three checkboxes, Replace All in: radio buttons (Whole document, Selection only), status line, buttons Find Next (default), Replace, Replace All, Close; access keys f n p r c a w o x d s. Enter in Find what = Find Next (default button); Enter in Replace with = Replace (its own `activate`); Esc closes; no Find Previous button and no Shift+Enter in this window (reversed in 8d, see below). `open()` calls `capture_selection()` first, then applies a one-line selection as the search text, and sets the search controller's status callback; `hide()` (no `Host::closed`) and `close()` remove it, so the two windows never fight over the single slot. The scope radio re-captures the current document selection when Selection only is chosen; on window activation only the greying and the radio state are refreshed (not the range), so Find Next inside the window never loses a remembered range. Known quirk: choosing Whole document and then Selection only again takes whatever is selected at that moment (often a single match). Status line: the Replace window shows `ReplaceController::status()` (pattern problem, then replacement problem, then the search status), bold through the same `vm-find-problem` class (CSS twin `window.vm-replace`).
- 2026-11-16 — (Orchestrator, design; Adam can veto) **STEP 8c, the wiring.** Both windows are plain `GtkWindow`s, so the app's `win.` accelerators do not reach them: Ctrl+H in the Find window and Ctrl+F in the Replace window are handled by the capture-phase key controller each window already has, which calls a `Host` callback (`show_replace`, `show_find`). Switching passes `std::nullopt` to `open()`, so the shared search text (maybe a regex) is not replaced by the selected match; opening from the main window passes the one-line selection, as Find does. The single status-callback slot of `SearchController` belongs to the window that is shown: each window sets it in `open()` and clears it in `hide()`, guarded by `listening_` (the Find window set it in its constructor until 8c). `hide()` never calls `Host::closed`; `close()` is `hide()` plus `Host::closed`. `MainWindow::open_find`/`open_replace` hide the other window first. The 8c prompt is exact text edits (23 KB) rather than a dictated file.
- 2026-11-16 — (Adam) **Replace window hands-on test: 24 of 24 PASS** (opening and switching with Ctrl+H and Ctrl+F, Find Next with wrap, Replace, Replace All as one undo step, regex groups, Selection only, Ctrl+C/X/V/A in both entries, access keys, tabs, last tab closed). **Decision reversed:** the Replace window gets a Find Previous button after all (8b had left it out to keep the window compact), with Shift+Enter in "Find what" as in the Find window; built in STEP 8d (access key `v`).
- 2026-11-16 — (Orchestrator, design; Adam can veto) **STEP 9, Go to Line, is split in two.** 9a is core and display-free: `goto_column_offset` makes the column of `line:column` mean the column the status bar shows (characters from 1, a tab counts as the columns it covers), so what Adam types is what he reads; `ui/line_jump` counts the lines, gives the range text and the current line, and `jump_to_line` moves the cursor (a column past the end of the line goes to its end; a line past the end goes to the start of the last line with the core's message). 9b is the window: a small plain `GtkWindow` titled "Go to Line", independent of the Find and Replace windows (not part of the one-at-a-time rule, no part in the shared search), one entry prefilled with the current line and selected, a label with the allowed range, a status line, buttons Go (default) and Cancel with access keys; Enter jumps and closes and the focus returns to the text; Esc closes; a line past the end jumps and the message "Line 99,999 does not exist; went to the last line, 12,408." goes to the main status bar; text that is not a line stays in the window with "Type a line number, or line:column, for example 120:8." in the problem style. The range follows the active tab when the window is activated.
- 2026-11-16 — (Adam) **Current-line highlight: always on, light blue `#DCE6F5`.** Adam asked for it like gedit's. It is always on for now (no menu item); a check box comes with the Settings window at M2.5. The `classic` scheme's `#F0F0F0` was imperceptible on one of his three monitors; he chose E (`#DCE6F5`) of five candidates, with C (`#E0E0E0`) a close second. He wants "a few presets and a couple defaults (light, dark)" when the settings menus are made (M2.5); the dark twin is built on `classic-dark` when the dark palette arrives. Adam looked at the band after STEP 9d and said it looks good.
- 2026-11-16 — (Orchestrator, from STEP 9d's test run and Adam's choice) **How the scheme reaches the editor.** GtkSourceView 5.12 treats custom style-scheme search paths as plain file-system paths; a `resource:///` folder does not load ("Failed to open file"), only its own built-in schemes load from resources by an internal route. So `vermilion-classic.xml` (`parent-scheme="classic"`, only the two current-line styles changed) is kept as a resource and written at start-up by `write_scheme_file()` to `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/vermilion/styles/vermilion-classic.xml` (folder mode 0700, written atomically with `g_file_set_contents`, and only when missing or different); that folder is added to the style-scheme manager's search path once; a failure logs a `g_warning` and the editor uses `classic`. The tests run with a temporary `XDG_CACHE_HOME`, so they never touch the real cache. Adam chose this option ("write it at start-up") over a user-visible data folder. ARCHITECTURE.md section 8 gets a row for it at the docs sweep.
- 2026-11-16 — (Adam) **M2.3 STEP 10 open questions answered: keep today's behaviour.** (a) The Find, Replace and Go to Line windows stay open when the last document closes (their buttons do nothing and do not crash; Find, Find Next, Find Previous, Replace and Go to Line are greyed out in the menu). (b) No Ctrl+G inside the Find and Replace windows and no Ctrl+F or Ctrl+H inside Go to Line. (c) The backlog item "Edit commands into the focused entry" is closed: Ctrl+C, X, V and A work in the entries of all three windows, which have no menu bar.
- 2026-11-16 — (Orchestrator, design from topics 4 and 6, built in STEP 10; Adam can veto) **F3 and Shift+F3.** Menu, action and shortcut act on the shared `SearchController`; before anything has been searched (the shared text is empty) they open Find, taking a one-line selection as its text like Ctrl+F. Because the Find and Replace windows are plain `GtkWindow`s (the app's `win.` accelerators do not reach them), each handles F3 and Shift+F3 in its own capture-phase key controller. With the windows closed the result goes to the main status bar: the controller's status, "Not found" when nothing matched, and nothing while the match count is still being scanned (no position can be shown yet).
- 2026-11-16 — (Orchestrator) **STEP 13a design and lessons.** The run reuses `replace_loop` (a match limit and a deadline checked after every 16th match) and keeps its position as a text MARK between slices (edits invalidate iterators); the undo step is opened by `begin_replace_run` and closed by `end_replace_run` or the destructor. Lessons: a prompt's closing code fence must sit on its own line (the generator now asserts it); a test that edited one 15,000-character line 5,000 times took over 60 s (iterators cost time in proportion to the line length), so tests that need many edits use short lines, and a minified file with one huge line is slow in the product too (the long-line guard is 350,000 characters; it is in the hands-on test).
- 2026-11-16 — (Adam) **Replace All gets slices, both scopes.** Adam ran Replace All ("e" to "E") on the 50 MB `big.cpp` spike and the library's synchronous `replace_all` froze the window so long that he killed the program (STEP 11's log line never printed). Decisions (answers to three questions): (1) during a sliced run the document is locked (read-only) and **Cancel** (Esc or a button) stops it; (2) Cancel **keeps what is replaced so far**, as one undo step (status: replaced N matches, stopped); (3) **both scopes** (Whole document and Selection only) use the same sliced loop, so the library's `replace_all` is no longer used for Replace All. The STEP 12 probe has answered the open points (full table in `claude/M2.3_reference_3.md`): the library's `replace_all` and plain buffer edits cost about 2.6 and 2.9 microseconds per match, a per-match `gtk_source_search_context_replace` about 23 (nine times more), all linear up to 96,000 matches; highlighting on or off makes no difference; a 500-match slice through the context takes about 11 ms and does not grow during the run; **one user action can stay open across idle turns and one `gtk_text_buffer_undo` undoes all of it**; memory in the undo history is roughly 0.25 KB per replaced match (to be measured in the app). Not measured: a view attached, syntax highlighting, the 50 MB file.
- 2026-11-16 (night) — (Orchestrator, design; Adam can veto) **STEP 13 became five prompts, 13a to 13e, and `ReplaceController` got `start_replace_all()`.** The first plan of 13b was a 50 KB prompt, so it was split: 13b the prerequisites, 13c the job, 13d the window wiring, 13e the deletion. The synchronous `replace_all()` stays, guarded, until 13e. `SearchController` gained `set_on_buffer_changing` (a running job ends first and keeps what it replaced) and `set_status_frozen` (no status recomputation per edit; one refresh at the end). The order in `finish_job` matters (stop the timer, end the run, restore the cursor, log, reset the job, show the message, unfreeze, call back) so `status()` and `job_active()` are right inside the callbacks. 13c was 47.7 KB (18.6 KB of dictated tests) and passed on Adam's machine, including the cursor and one-undo-step tests that could not run in the cloud.
- 2026-11-16 (night) — (Adam noticed it; the Orchestrator wrote it) **The size numbers of AGENTS.md rule 16 are ceilings, not targets.** Qwen's test files cluster just under the trigger (493, 489 and 465 lines against 500) and its reports say "under the limit" without asking whether the file is still one responsibility. Rule 16 got one sentence: a file past about 80% of its trigger (240 lines, 400 for tests) gets one line in the report on its one responsibility. The Orchestrator's own slip the same night: it called a 465-line test file close to the split point; for tests the trigger is 500 and the split 800.
- 2026-11-16 (night) — (Adam) **STEP 13d is ONE prompt, whatever its size.** The Orchestrator had offered to split it if it passed 40 KB; Adam said not to split it. It came to 38 KB.
- 2026-11-16 (night) — (Orchestrator, design; Adam can veto) **How the job locks the window (13d).** The page is locked with the view's own editable flag (restored at the unlock, so a read-only page stays read-only); `busy()` includes the lock, so Save, Save As, Reopen and the format commands are already greyed. `HeldEvents` holds back the page's `edited` and `cursor` callbacks during a job (a job edits thousands of times a second; the status bar column of a long line and the recovery copies would cost more than the edits) and passes each on once at the unlock. In the Replace window the Replace All button becomes Cancel (access key A stays) and has the focus, the other controls are insensitive, Esc and the title-bar close cancel first and the next Esc closes, F3, Shift+F3 and Ctrl+F do nothing. In the main window Undo, Redo, Cut, Paste, Find, Find Next, Find Previous and Word Wrap are greyed during a job; **Replace stays enabled and only brings the Replace window forward; Go to Line stays available** (it only moves the cursor); closing a tab, Close Tab and Quit end the job first (the close may save a locked document). The job's done callback can run inside `SearchController::set_buffer()`, so `replace_job_ended()` calls `update_state()`, never `refresh()`. Open: whether Ctrl+Z reaches the view's own binding during a job (Adam's hands-on test).
- 2026-11-16 (late night) — (Adam) **STEP 13e is ONE prompt, and the limit is now 65 KB.** "Step 13e, please make it one prompt, do not break it up. Our new limit is 65kb before breaking." 13e came to 51 KB.

- 2026-11-16 (late night) — (Orchestrator, design; Adam can veto) **How 13e deletes the old path.** The field `ReplaceAllResult::refused` goes too (only the two deleted functions set it), so the two `EXPECT_FALSE(result.refused)` lines in the run tests go; `ReplaceAllResult` stays with `count` only because `end_replace_run()` returns it. The tests that used the deleted functions are ported, not dropped: the session tests replace through a helper that does what the job does all at once, the controller tests start the job and fire a fake timer. Fix (13f): a capture-phase key controller on the view that stops Ctrl+Z, Ctrl+Y and Ctrl+Shift+Z while `held_.holding()`. Retest passed. This answers the open question of 13d.
