**Standing process facts**: accepted design, not debt: `value_or` needs copyable `T`; `key=` query parameters are deliberately not masked; rule A's authorization branch stops only at quotes, comma, CR and LF. GLib 2.80 `g_object_add_weak_pointer` does not set the pointer on add (tests preset it); reports show raw failing build output first; `git diff` shows nothing for untracked files, so prompts ask for new files in full; prompts keep every dictated C++ line within 100 columns; Qwen replaced raw output with summaries twice early on, answered by AGENTS.md rule 18 (evidence files the Orchestrator reads itself and checks for staleness by header and mtime); clang-format is 18.1.3 on Adam's machine and not installed in the Orchestrator's VM (the cloud workspace has it; the project's `.clang-format` must be next to the file when checking, or use `--assume-filename`); do not run dependent Orchestrator tool calls in parallel; check clang-format option names against version 18 before dictating them; GTK 4 has no tray icon. Never read a member of an object in the same call that moves the object into a lambda capture (argument evaluation order is unspecified; this broke STEP 5f on Adam's GCC and not in the Orchestrator's compilers); a hands-on test of every UI step by Adam follows its audit. Prompt-authoring practices are in AI_ONBOARDING.md section 2 item 8. **Probes (M2.3):** when a library's behaviour is not documented, the Orchestrator writes a small dictated program under `evidence/<gate>/probe/` (git-ignored) that PRINTS what it observes (unbuffered output, `timeout` on the run, no asserts, no interpretation by Qwen); the Orchestrator reads the output and records the facts in a reference doc before designing on them. Check every GtkSourceView or GTK function name against the installed headers on Adam's machine before dictating it (neither the cloud nor the device shell VM has the GtkSourceView headers, so dictate only names that the compiled probes in `evidence/M2.3/probe/` or the committed code already use, or add a read-only grep step; a syntax check against a stub proves nothing about names: STEP 3 dictated four `gtk_source_buffer_*undo*` functions that do not exist). GLib's error texts on Adam's machine equal the cloud's (`... at char N: reason`); GtkSourceView's own regex error text has another shape and is never shown. **Dictating GTK calls (6b lesson):** when a prompt names a call with iterator or mark arguments or out-parameters, it gives the exact signature (`gtk_text_buffer_get_iter_at_mark(buffer, &iter, mark)`: iterator second, mark third) and says what each iterator holds after the call (after `gtk_source_search_context_replace` `match_start` is the START of the replacement and `match_end` its END); a spec sentence like "the iterator the library updated" is ambiguous and cost a fix loop.
**Open decisions (Adam's call)**: how to distribute the alpha binaries (an `install.sh` that checks the OS, installs dependencies, clones, builds and installs; a `.deb`; a PPA): Adam is undecided and leans to one `install.sh` for the three programs; the Orchestrator's view is a prebuilt release zip with `install.sh` for the alpha and `.deb` packages at the beta, to settle at M9 (no hurry). Otherwise none. Names of config files and JSON schemas are fixed when first written (ARCHITECTURE.md section 10); gdb DAP reliability is checked at M7.2; packaging details at M9 (ARCHITECTURE.md 7.12 and the Backlog below).
**Small leftovers (not debt)** (file lengths of 2026-11-17; review line 300, split line 500, tests 500 and 800): the dictated tracker tests carry unused `using` declarations (`Ids` in `recovery_tracker_test.cpp`; `LineEnding`, `TextEncoding`, `RecoveryJob`, `kCopied` in `recovery_tracker_rules_test.cpp`; harmless; clean up if those files are edited again); the reopen question's detail text says "Your unsaved changes will be lost." although it offers Save and Reopen; `replace_window.cpp` 471 lines, `document_tabs.cpp` 443, `editor_page.cpp` 404 (M2.4 step 7), `main_window.cpp` 323, `external_change_controller.cpp` 344 (8a; all over the review line; look at splits when next edited or at the M2 review); test files near their trigger: `tests/replace_job_test.cpp` 516, `recovery_prompt_test.cpp` 493, `replace_controller_test.cpp` 467, `search_controller_test.cpp` 465; help window labels are not selectable (a follow-up); the CSS class `vm-help-bullet` has no rule.

## 2. Decisions
**Where decisions live (the live copies).** Product, stack and rails: ARCHITECTURE.md sections 1 to 5 and AI_ONBOARDING.md section 3. Process, gates, prompts, audits: AI_ONBOARDING.md section 2 and AGENTS.md. The UI (look, menus, tabs, status bar): ARCHITECTURE.md 7.10. Crash recovery: 7.11. Three programs, settings, packaging: 7.12 and roadmap M9. ctxmon: 7.7 and roadmap M6. Gates, progress weights, dogfood markers: `docs/roadmap.md`. Unscheduled ideas: `docs/ideas.md`. Any older decision and who decided it: `claude/HANDOFF_ARCHIVE.md`, Part 1.

**Standing decisions that have no other home**
- **CrimsonEditor reference clone** (Adam, 2026-11-13): `/home/adam/Projects/CrimsonEditor` is a clone of https://github.com/igkang00/CrimsonEditor (MIT licence, `main` at `75d3567`, 2026-11-08), kept so code can be taken from it. **No chat requests access to it any more (Adam, 2026-11-16: the request was denied by the permission classifier and he asked that sessions stop asking); if code from it is ever needed, the Orchestrator tells Adam and he attaches the folder or carries the file across.** It is read-only for the Orchestrator and the agent: the Orchestrator reads it, never edits it, and no agent writes there. Code taken from it keeps its MIT notice in the borrowing file (their copyright line and the MIT licence text beside our SPDX header; the wording is fixed at the first gate that imports something), and borrowed code is reviewed like any other and must obey the project's rails (C++17, GTK 4, no exceptions, layers). The device shell cannot reach GitHub (proxy 403); a clone or update is made in the cloud workspace and carried across as one archive, or Adam runs `git pull` himself.
- **One step per prompt, a prompt up to 65 KB** (Adam, 2026-11-11 at 25 KB; raised to 40 KB on 2026-11-16; **raised to 65 KB on 2026-11-16 (night): "our new limit is 65 KB before breaking"**; the 40 KB reasoning: the 2026-11-11 hang was CLion/llama.cpp, and a 25 KB prompt's evidence run ended at 52k of 131k tokens; the 51 KB three-step history below is why it is still one step per prompt) : the M2.2b prompt of three steps (51 KB, 69k tokens in Qwen's session) hung the agent. Each prompt is self-contained (own build commands, own evidence list, own "Last verified" line listing every uncommitted file); a step with a large dictated test is split further. This supersedes the multi-step wording of AI_ONBOARDING.md 2.3 (corrected 2026-11-12). Anything for Adam to copy that is over 5 KB (every prompt) is sent as a file with SendUserFile, never pasted inline; shorter text goes inline (Adam, 2026-11-12; this replaces the 2026-11-08 "inline" rule). SendUserFile writes a copy into the repo folder as `Claude outputs/` (now excluded in `.git/info/exclude`; delete it if it shows in `git status`).
- **All work stays inside the repo and its gated steps** (Adam, 2026-11-11): no throwaway programs or scratch builds on his machine; a measurement is logged by the code itself (`G_MESSAGES_DEBUG=vermilion`) and read from the log. The Orchestrator's checks in the cloud workspace are not affected.
- **Security policy pinned** (Adam, 2026-11-08, updated 2026-11-10): no SECURITY.md, security section or untrusted-input rule until agentic coding exists and a version is released; the SECURITY.md work is done before the 1.0.0 beta starts. The plan and open questions are in the archive (decision log, 2026-11-08 "Security policy pinned").
- **Gate numbers**: the roadmap was restructured on 2026-11-11; the mapping from the old numbers (used in the archive and in older Project docs) is at the top of the archive. **2026-11-17 (Adam, executive decisions):** the diff is gate M2.6 (the agent milestone's diff engine and DiffView, old M6.5 and M6.11, moved there; their numbers are not reused); the old M2.6, M2.6b and M2.6c are M2.7, M2.7b and M2.7c; milestones M5 and M6 swapped (M5 is the agent core, M6 the ctxmon UI); versions follow the milestones (roadmap, Versions). Entries dated earlier use the old numbers and labels (the archive's mapping).
- **Prompts and AGENTS.md never mention what the Orchestrator checks or reads** (Adam, 2026-11-06). Engineering options are discussed with Adam, not decided for him (AI_ONBOARDING.md 2.9).

**Active-gate decisions (the M2.4 design talk, from 2026-11-17, and that day's roadmap changes; M2.3's design and build decisions, 2026-11-13 to 2026-11-17, are in the archive, Part 22; earlier ones in Parts 7 to 15)**
- 2026-11-17 — (Orchestrator's design while writing step 8a, Adam can correct it) **Step 8 is split: 8a builds the logic, 8b the wiring.** `ExternalChangeController` (`src/ui/external_change_controller.{h,cpp}`) owns the one `FileWatcher` (active tab only) and the check routine `check(DocumentId)`; it reaches the pages only through an abstract `ExternalChangeHost`, so its 34 tests run without a display against real files. Rules built in: a check waits while the page is not quiet (loading, saving, locked, recording its baseline) and runs at `settled(id)` or `follow_active()`; a check never starts twice for one id; an answer is dropped when the document, its page or its baseline changed meanwhile; every action except ShowBanner takes a banner down; a silent reload scans the new content first (a NUL byte or a very long line makes it a banner, whose Reload asks "reload anyway"); the user's edits during the scan turn a silent reload into a banner; `DocumentRecord::encoding_chosen` (set by Reopen as, step 8b) keeps the user's encoding across a reload, a detected one is announced when it changed (`reloaded_encoding_message`); the recovery tracker gives a deleted document a copy (`needs_save()`). **Orchestrator-side check method:** the repo sources are tarred on the device into `evidence/M2.4/orch/` (git-ignored; the tarball may be deleted by Adam), staged to the cloud workspace and built there with a small gtest shim (`/home/claude/w`, lost with the workspace; GLib and GIO only, no GTK headers).
- 2026-11-17 — (Orchestrator's reading while writing step 7, Adam can correct it) **“Keep mine” on a file that came back after a deletion must leave the document needing a save** (its text may differ from the file, so the unsaved mark stays); on a changed file it accepts the disk's time, size and hash as the baseline (topic 2). **Step 7 built the parts without wiring them:** a monitor event is never inspected for its kind (every event means “look again”, debounced 300 ms; `G_FILE_MONITOR_NONE`, no `WATCH_MOVES`); the banner is a plain GTK box with a label and two buttons (`GtkInfoBar` is deprecated), classic tooltip yellow `vm_info_bg #ffffe1`; the editor page widget is now a vertical box (banner above the scrolled view) and `load()` hides the banner.
- 2026-11-17 — (Adam, 22:20: “can you make a script for me? … `./build --light`”) **`./vbuild` at the repo root** builds the programs: `--light` (target `vermilion`), `--ide` (`vermilion-ide`), `--ctxmon` (`ctxmon`; both not written yet, the script says so), `--all`, `--debug` (Debug build in `build/debug`), `--test` (ctest), `-j N`. Named `vbuild` because a file `build` cannot sit beside the CMake folder `build/`, which the gate prompts and AGENTS.md use. REUSE header in the file; untracked until Adam commits it.
- 2026-11-17 — (Adam, 12:16: "mark it down as an objective, and going forward make sure everything we generate is REUSE compliant, during the M9 milestone we will get ourselves REUSE compliant.") **REUSE certification is a standing objective, reached at M9.** From now on everything created is compliant when it is made: AGENTS.md rule 5 now covers every new file of any kind (source, scripts and CMake carry the two lines in their comment syntax; Markdown carries them as two HTML comments on the first lines; JSON, fixtures and images get a `<name>.license` sidecar; `third_party/` is never edited and has sidecars), and the Orchestrator applies it to its own new files (manual topics, milestone reviews, plans: `docs/milestones/M2.4-plan.md` got its header at 12:16). Files made before 2026-11-17 are not touched until M9, when every one gets its header or a `REUSE.toml` annotation, `reuse lint` passes and goes into CI. Written down in AGENTS.md rule 5, AI_ONBOARDING.md (2.8 and rail 3.1), the roadmap (a standing-objective paragraph and the M9 paragraph). Open for M9, not now: licence and holder for docs, fixtures and artwork, whether `reference/ctxmon` is GPL-3.0-or-later, and `REUSE.toml` against sidecars (the Orchestrator recommends `REUSE.toml`; the sidecars made until then are folded into it). Declined for now: the repo-wide step before the next gate commit.
- 2026-11-17 — (Adam, 12:11, after placing xxHash: "It is done, but we need to make an exception to the rules about touching them. I am hoping to get REUSE certification on the repo, and that means we need to make sure SPDX headers and a couple other things are in order; please make sure these files follow said rules and if not, we need to implement it.") **Goal: REUSE certification for the whole repository** (every file carries copyright and licence information the REUSE tool can read; the licence texts are in `LICENSES/`). **Exception (Adam):** the Orchestrator may add REUSE metadata outside its usual docs-only editing rights (sidecar `.license` files, `REUSE.toml`, `LICENSES/`, headers in docs), including under `third_party/`; it still never edits vendored code, so `xxhash.h` stays byte-exact. **Done at 12:11 for the vendored files:** xxHash v0.8.3 confirmed (version define; sums in the plan, step 1); the header has a copyright line and the BSD text but no SPDX tag, so it got the sidecar `third_party/xxhash/xxhash.h.license` (`SPDX-FileCopyrightText: 2012-2023 Yann Collet`, `SPDX-License-Identifier: BSD-2-Clause`); `LICENSES/GPL-3.0-or-later.txt` (a copy of the root `LICENSE`, GPL-3, 35,149 bytes) and `LICENSES/BSD-2-Clause.txt` (the SPDX list text, sha256 `f32fb3b4…`) were added; `third_party/xxhash/README.md` was rewritten with an in-file header and a correction: **the v0.8.3 `LICENSE` holds the BSD-2 text of the library only, not the GPLv2 text of `xxhsum` as the Orchestrator had said** (the plan and the README now say so). `LICENSE` files are ignored by the REUSE tool by name. **Survey of the rest of the repo (391 tracked files; a grep for the SPDX line, so a doc that only mentions it counts as covered):** all source files carry the SPDX line and the copyright line (AGENTS.md rule 5); about 175 tracked text files do not: 140 under `tests/ctxmon/` (fixtures: logs, JSON, `drm` data files), 9 manual topics, 3 milestone reviews and the other docs (`README.md`, `HANDOFF.md`, `AI_ONBOARDING.md`, `docs/roadmap.md`, `docs/ideas.md`, `docs/handoff-archive.md`, `docs/requirements`), 3 files under `res/vedt/` (artwork), 2 under `reference/ctxmon/` (Adam's Python original), the CMake files (about 7), `.clang-format`, `.gitignore`, `opencode.json`, and the screenshots in `res/github/` (binary). They need headers or `REUSE.toml` annotations (one annotation can cover a whole folder, which is the practical way for fixtures and images). **The repo-wide step is not done:** it waits for Adam's decisions (the licence and holder for docs, fixtures and the artwork; whether `reference/ctxmon` is GPL-3.0-or-later; whether `REUSE.toml` or sidecars) and his `reuse lint` output, because the `reuse` tool is not installed in the Orchestrator's workspaces and cannot be installed there. **Decided at 12:16: the repo-wide work is done at M9** (entry above).

- 2026-11-17 — (Adam, M2.4 design talk, topic 5, "B, but if the user closes the application it does a check on all tabs"; confirmed "Correct" to the Orchestrator's reading) **Only the active tab has a file monitor; a background tab is checked when the user switches to it** (time and size first, then the hash if they differ; the tab shows at once, and a silent reload or the banner follows a moment later if the file really changed). **At quit every tab gets the cheap check:** a file that is gone marks its tab as having unsaved changes (topic 3), so the quit question asks; a dirty tab whose file also changed on disk gets one extra line in its quit question ("this file also changed on disk") and Save then asks the usual Save Anyway; a clean tab whose file changed needs nothing (no reload of a tab about to close). The same check runs when a single background tab is closed with its close button (the Orchestrator's addition, accepted with the reading). Network drives (no file-monitor events) are caught only at a tab switch or at quit. **Debounce (Orchestrator default, not objected to): about 300 ms after the last event,** through the injectable `DebounceTimer` (the one Replace All and recovery use; a fake in tests); a file still missing after the wait counts as deleted. If a monitor cannot be created, the switch check still works. Declined: A (every open tab watched live) and C (every tab live plus a re-check when the window regains focus).
- 2026-11-17 — (Adam, M2.4 design talk, topic 6, "We will do B") **Large files: above 10 MB a clean tab does not reload by itself; the banner (Reload, Ignore) appears instead.** At 10 MB or below it reloads silently (topic 1). **The threshold is fixed at 10 MB; no tuning by hand-testing other sizes (Adam: "10 is set").** One named constant; the size is the file's size on disk, which the document already records (the Orchestrator reads 10 MB as 10 MiB, 10,485,760 bytes; the plan states it). Orchestrator's defaults for read-only documents, built unless Adam objects: a binary or escaped (read-only) tab is always clean and follows the same size rule; a reload scans the new content as an open does, and if the result would trigger an open-time question (a NUL byte, a line over 350,000 characters) the reload is not silent: the banner appears and the question is asked when the user presses Reload; an encoding chosen with Reopen as is kept across reloads, an automatically detected one is detected again and the status bar says so if it changes. - 2026-11-17 — (Adam, M2.4 design talk, topic 3, "A") **A file that is deleted or moved while its tab is open: the tab and its text stay, and the document is marked as having unsaved changes** (so closing asks Save / Close Without Saving, and the crash-recovery copy covers it). The status bar says "<name> was deleted or moved on disk". Save writes the file again at the same path with no "changed on disk" question (nothing to conflict with). If the file comes back and the user has not edited the tab since, it reloads silently (topic 1); if the user has, the banner appears (topic 2). A move or rename counts as a delete: the editor does not follow it. A file is called deleted only if it is still missing after the debounce (topic 5): a save by write-and-rename (git, other editors) is an ordinary change. Needs its own flag (deleted on disk) next to the real modified flag, so a deletion alone is not mistaken for the user's edits. Declined: B (a banner with Save and Close Tab) and C (close the tab on its own: it breaks the no-auto-close rule and loses the text).
- 2026-11-17 — (Adam, M2.4 design talk, topic 4; the hash is Adam's idea) **Our own saves are told from outside changes by the file's modification time and size, and then by a hash of the file's bytes.** Each document records time, size and hash after every load, reload and own save. A monitor event whose time and size match the record is ignored without reading. If they differ, the file is read and hashed (off the main loop, on GIO's thread pool): an equal hash means nothing really changed (a `touch`, a formatter rewriting identical text), so only the recorded time is updated, with no reload and no banner; a different hash is an outside modification (a silent reload for a clean buffer, the banner for a dirty one). The first hash comes from the scanner's existing whole-file pass on open; after our own save the file is read back once. "Keep mine" (topic 2) records the disk's new time, size and hash. All behind one function, `hash_file`. Known limits: a tool that rewrites a file with the same size and restores the old time is missed by the fast path; an outside change between our save and the read-back would be recorded as ours (tiny window). Declined: time and size only, a compare of the file with the buffer text (encodings and line endings make it awkward), ignoring events for a moment after a save (can hide a real change).
- 2026-11-17 — (Adam, topic 4) **New dependency, vendored: xxHash (XXH3).** Decided "Vendor it" over GLib's GChecksum (slower, no dependency) and over the system package (`libxxhash-dev` 0.8.2 is in Ubuntu 24.04 main; it would need the README, CI and CMake package lines). The library is BSD-2-Clause (the `xxhsum` tool is GPLv2 and is not used), compatible with GPL-3-or-later; its notice and licence text stay with the file. Plan: `third_party/xxhash/xxhash.h` and `LICENSE` at the repo root (outside `src`, `tests` and `tools`, which `check_sources.sh` and `check_format.sh` scan), byte-exact and untouched; **Adam places the files** (a pinned release; the Orchestrator cannot fetch a byte-exact copy and never retypes it); the gate records the version and the file's sha256; one `.cpp` defines `XXH_INLINE_ALL` and includes it as a system header (so `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` do not apply to it); Qwen never edits `third_party/`. Docs sweep of the gate: ARCHITECTURE.md section 6 (dependency table, "bundled") and section 9 (do not touch), the M9 NOTICE entry. The M2.4 plan makes the vendoring its own step with Adam's part named.

## 2b. Coding Agent Profiles  *(edit as evidence accumulates)*
**Agent A: Qwen3.8-27B (local llama.cpp) — the llama.cpp coding agent for this project (confirmed by Adam 2026-11-04).** Setup as in WeatherAPP: Q4_K_M via llama.cpp (Vulkan, MTP drafting). In that project its profile was: accurate facts and line numbers, discloses omissions, unaltered raw blocks; weaknesses were soft classifications, verbosity, and occasionally "..." inside raw blocks. Re-profile on this codebase; its habits on C/C++ and GTK are unknown.
- Gate H0 (2026-11-04): PASS. Notes: wrote "(no output)" for empty command output instead of an empty raw block; chat paste lost code fences, so fencing was not verified.
- M0.2 to M0.5b2 (2026-11-05/06): six gates in a row PASS WITH NOTES, no FAIL. Raw outputs matched the Orchestrator's fresh re-runs every time; it reports prompt bugs honestly instead of silently working around them; recurring small slips are test-count arithmetic in prose and "(no output)" annotations for empty output. M0.5b2 report was clean (all proofs matched).
- M2.3 STEPs 13b and 13c (2026-11-16): PASS and PASS WITH NOTES, checked against the files on disk. Quirks: it turns raw output into tables or prose (so the Orchestrator reads the evidence files and hashes itself; Adam: "it's back and forth, give and take", Qwen also catches the Orchestrator's slips, rule 22); it printed two full sha256 lines with a corrupted tail (typed, not pasted); it wrote "under the 300-line trigger" about a 465-line file and did not flag a 518-line test file (it treats a limit as a target); in a fresh session it found the edits already applied and stopped at the hash gate with a correct Prompt doubts entry (good).
**Agent B: none.** Gemini Flash (Antigravity `agy acp`) was set up and passed a read-only smoke test (Gate A0), then dropped by Adam on 2026-11-04. Not used, not offered. Gemma remains permanently banned.
**Gemma: permanently banned (Adam).** Never use, suggest, benchmark, or prompt for it.
**Harness (from 2026-11-04)**: OpenCode 1.19.5 (`~/.opencode/bin/opencode`, ACP) launched by CLion's Air plugin via `~/.jetbrains/acp.json` (entry "OpenCode", args `acp`, both MCP options off). Versions: CLion 2026.2.4.1 (snap), Air plugin 262.8665.521, Agent Client Protocol plugin 262.11879.106. Model wiring is in the project-root `opencode.json`: provider `llama.cpp` at http://127.0.0.1:8080/v1, model key `qwen3.8-27b`, limits context 131072 / output 8192 (match `qwen-server` -c and -n). Permissions in the same file (opt-out policy from 2026-11-05): edit = allow; webfetch and websearch = deny; bash = `"*": "allow"` first, then deny for each of `git push`, `commit`, `cherry-pick`, `merge`, `rebase`, `revert`, `am`, `checkout`, `switch`, `restore`, `reset`, `clean`, `stash`, `update-ref`, written both as `git <cmd>*` and `git -* <cmd>*` (the second form catches `git -C dir <cmd>`; it is narrower than `git * <cmd>*`, which wrongly denied `git grep -n commit`). OpenCode: the last matching rule wins. Qwen can do anything else without a prompt; only Adam commits and pushes. **OpenCode's defaults are mostly allow, so never remove the permission block.** Verified: agent reads AGENTS.md, answers correctly, ends with the required phrase; `ls` prompted for approval (before the widening; `ls` no longer prompts). Not yet verified: that denying a prompt is honored, behaviour over multi-step gates, and behaviour after a context wipe. Observed quirk: command output displayed twice in the chat view (display only). The Air plugin's docs do not describe permissions for custom agents, so keep re-checking the approval prompts after plugin updates.
**Prompt sizing**: ~10 hits needing context per gate; counts-only for high-volume sweeps; at most 10 commands per tool call.

- (Closed 2026-11-16, Adam: Ctrl+C, X, V and A work in the entries of the Find, Replace and Go to Line windows; nothing is left of the M2.1 "Edit commands into the focused entry" item.)
- Distribution of the alpha (Adam, 2026-11-14): see Open decisions. M9 already plans `.deb` packages, a release zip and `install.sh`; the open question is whether the first alpha ships only as source plus `install.sh`.
- Search polish ideas (not scheduled): a clearer message when a pattern can only match the empty string; whole-word wrapping does not help a pattern whose last character is a non-word character.
- **Known limit of M2.3 (documented in the manual):** highlighting every match on one very long line can freeze the window (GTK drawing errors); the cost of Undo for a huge Replace All is about four times the run's work and cannot be cancelled. Revisit only if either bites in daily use (the options are in the decision of 2026-11-17 above).
- **OpenCode usage log (Adam, 2026-11-14):** read OpenCode's logs or session data read-only (its data lives under `~/.opencode` and the XDG folders; the Orchestrator reads with `device_bash`, no throwaway programs) to see which tools Qwen really calls over recent gates and how often `edit` fails to match; the result informs gap 1 and the thirteen-tool list.
- **M5 plan question: freshness of buffers the agent touches (external review, Qwen4-Max, 2026-11-17; two notes from Adam).** M2.4 watches only the active tab, but a staged agent edit to a closed file opens a background tab, and the agent's tools act on buffers. The M2.4 controller's check routine is keyed by document id (plan step 8), so the agent can call it. The M5 plan settles: (1) **which tools** run the check, enumerated, not just `read_file` and edits: `read_file`, `search` (it reads open buffers), `replace_text`, `save_file`, `create_file` with overwrite, `file_ops`; (2) **semantics**: a clean buffer with an outside change reloads silently (M2.4 topic 1: undo history CLEARED, not one undo step; ARCHITECTURE.md 7.6 "External changes" still says "one undo step" and is corrected in the M2.4 docs sweep), an edited buffer (every buffer after the agent's first edit) makes the tool fail with a plain message the agent can read (fail-closed, as the staged-edits rule for `save_file` already says, ARCHITECTURE.md 7.6, confirmed by the M5.3b plan); (3) **where**: the check is asynchronous (stat, then a hash on the thread pool) but the tool functions are synchronous, so the reviewer's proposal, to be checked against the loop design at M5.2, is to run the check in the loop BEFORE dispatching a tool call, reusing the loop's suspension point that `ask_user` needs, so every tool stays synchronous and the policy lives in one place, not in each tool. Not a decision yet.
- M5.3b plan questions: what an agent edit does when the buffer already holds the user's unsaved changes (it asks first); whether `replace_text` needs whitespace tolerance; what Accept writes to disk in M5.6b (per hunk, or once every hunk of a file is decided).
- M6.3: `Alert::level` becomes `int`. M7.3: breakpoints are kept by file in core, not in the buffer, so closing a tab does not lose them (there is no tab limit).
- M7: F10 is GTK's menu-bar key and the plan gives it to Step Over; check that this works. On XFCE the window manager may take Alt+F-keys (Alt+F8 for Find was considered and dropped, 2026-11-13).
- **File > Open Recent (Adam, 2026-11-16, asked during the Replace hands-on test): now the roadmap gate M2.7c (2026-11-17; it was M2.6c until the renumbering that day), after M2.7, before M2.7b.** A design talk with Adam comes first. ## 4. Session Log  *(newest first, keep 3; older entries go to the archive)*

### 2026-11-17 (23:50, local, fourteenth chat, handoff) — M2.4 step 8b written and issued
- Resumed from the thirteenth chat's handoff (no drift: HEAD `01093fd`, the same 54 changed paths). Adam confirmed the readback ("Continue"). **Step 8b was designed and written:** the pages get `quiet()`, `baseline()`, `set_baseline_stamp()` and a view position; the tabs get `reload()`, `mark_modified()`, `note_deleted_on_disk()` (in a new file, because `document_tabs.cpp` would pass 500 lines) and a `settled` callback; a thin host adapter implements `ExternalChangeHost`; the window creates the controller after the tabs, resets it first in its destructor, and calls `follow_active()` at the end of `refresh()`. Design points settled while reading the code: a reload clears the deleted flag at its start, so a failed load closes the tab like a failed open (known limit); `RecoveryController::document_modified(false)` now skips a document whose file is gone (found while tracing the flow: without it a deleted, clean tab lost its copy at the next modified-flag change); a small core function `reload_format()` carries the encoding rule (chosen encoding kept, the line ending taken from the scan only when the scan found the same encoding). The cloud workspace was rebuilt (gtest shim, sources tarred on the device into `evidence/M2.4/orch/`); the dictated code was compiled, run and mutation-tested there. The prompt is 47,764 bytes; Adam sends it to Qwen. Nothing committed; **the audit of the 8b report is next.**

### 2026-11-17 (23:40, local, thirteenth chat, handoff) — M2.4 step 8a PASS WITH NOTES
- Resumed from the twelfth chat's handoff (no drift, HEAD `01093fd`). Adam confirmed the readback ("Continue"). **Step 8 was split** (the controller's 34 tests alone are 24 KB): 8a builds the logic behind an abstract `ExternalChangeHost` (no display needed), 8b wires it in. The 8a prompt (59,287 bytes) was written, its dictated header and tests compiled and run in the cloud against a reference implementation (also against three broken variants, which the tests caught) before it went out; Adam uploaded it to the Project. **Qwen's report arrived 23:30 and was audited 23:40:** the four dictated hashes match, the evidence files are fresh (HEAD, times), 851 then 885 of 885 tests, 0 warnings, no stray files, no `~/.config/vermilion`; the `.cpp` was read line by line against the specification (notes in Next action (0)). Tooling note: `project_write` of a file by path needs the whole file read in one `Read` (limit 25,000 tokens, about 50 KB of this kind of text), so a 59 KB prompt cannot be mirrored by the Orchestrator; Adam uploads such prompts himself. Nothing committed; **step 8b is next.**

## Part 31: roadmap rewrite (2026-11-18), text moved out of docs/roadmap.md

The roadmap was rewritten to keep every line at 60 words or fewer. The texts below were removed or condensed there. They are kept verbatim.

### Old weights and weight history (old line 23)

**Weights**: M0 5 · M1 6 · M2 19 · M3 8 · M4 6 · M5 25 · M6 10 · M7 10 · M8 5 · M9 6 (sum 100). Reweighed again on 2026-11-17 (below); reweighed 2026-11-15 on Adam's proposal (the Orchestrator checked that it sums to 100 and kept it as proposed): the previous weights were M0 6 · M1 7 · M2 15 · M3 7 · M4 10 · M5 23 · M6 8 · M7 11 · M8 6 · M9 7. Share of one gate within its milestone: M0 0.42% (12 gates) · M1 0.55% (11) · M2 1.27% (15) · M3 1.33% (6) · M4 1.20% (5) · M5 1.67% (15) · M6 1.25% (8) · M7 1.67% (6) · M8 1.67% (3) · M9 6.00% (1). The finished milestones now weigh 11 (M0 5 plus M1 6), so the percentage fell from 21% to 20% with no gate undone. Earlier, on 2026-11-13, it was reweighed after the agent scope was cut to local first (the Orchestrator's estimate, Adam asked for it): the old weights were M0 5 · M1 6 · M2 13 · M3 6 · M4 9 · M5 25 · M6 7 · M7 10 · M8 12 · M9 7. The parked work (M5.12 summarising, the keyring, the heavy parts of change sets, the Anthropic and Gemini adapters, MCP, `.rpm` packages) was taken out of M5, M8 and M9 and the freed share was spread over all milestones in proportion to their old weights, so finished milestones now weigh a little more. **Reweighed on 2026-11-17** (Adam moved the diff from the agent milestone into M2 as gate M2.6, swapped M5 and M6, and asked for a reweigh if needed; the Orchestrator's estimate, Adam can correct it). The two moved gates carry about 3 points with them (the agent milestone's 28 points over 17 gates were 1.65 a gate, so two gates are about 3), so M2 goes from 16 to 19 and the agent core (M5, was M6) from 28 to 25; the ctxmon UI (M6, was M5) keeps its 10 through the swap and every other weight is unchanged (sum 100). Per-gate shares above are the new ones. Progress: 5 + 6 + 19 × 8/14 = 21.9, so 21% (it was 20%). The same day the Help window gate M2.5b was added (M2 now has 15 gates): 5 + 6 + 19 × 8/15 = 21.1, still 21%.

### Old footnotes of finished M2 gates

† 131 tests. Run as five steps in one prompt, then a fresh session for STEP 4b (fixes after Adam tried the tabs: fixed tab width, background close, left neighbour, Edit commands on the active editor) and STEP 5 with the menu colours; fix 1 shortened Help → About.

‡ 132 tests. Two steps (the rename, then rendering and use). Fix 1 after Adam opened About: GTK gives the focus to the program name when About opens, which selects it, so the stylesheet draws selected text in About without a highlight (Adam's option B over leaving it or moving the focus in code). On Adam's XFCE with Greybird the title bar shows the window-menu arrow, not an application icon, so the badge is used where XFCE asks for small icons.

¶ 132 tests. One step; the workflow was checked as YAML before it was issued and is first run by GitHub after the push.

§ 205 tests. Part A (core: scanner, formats, file documents, open messages; 165 then 181 tests), M2.2 UI (the open flow, STEPS 3 and 4; 185 tests), the save part (STEP 5 core, 205 tests; STEPS 6 and 7 the saver, the status bar menus, Save, Save As and the questions) and one fix (the dialogs start in the home folder; Save and Reopen). Adam ran 18 checks of open and save by hand; the byte checks all matched. Long-line limit 350,000 characters; saving 38.9 MB took 6.5 s.

‖ 259 tests. Six one-step prompts (the spans, the page blocks, the page history, the manual as a second resource with two tests about the real files, the help window, the wiring); Qwen wrote `manual_spans.cpp`, `manual_page.cpp`, `page_history.cpp` and `help_window.cpp` from specifications and passed audits with a differential fuzz against the Orchestrator's reference implementations; one fix (comments moved off statements, an unused include). The manual gained the topic "Using the manual".

\# 482 tests. Fifteen prompts in all (the format and rejection tests, the schedule, the store in two parts, the tracker, the hooks, the controller and its wiring, the working-again message, and STEP 5 in seven: the offer, the question texts, the document hooks, the answers, the file helpers, the prompt class, the window wiring); Qwen wrote the `.cpp` files from specs, headers, tests and wiring dictated. STEP 6 was Adam's run on a 52,428,800-character file: a copy holds the main loop 95 to 125 ms, a restore of the whole text takes 976 ms, nothing felt slow; the ceiling stays at 100,000,000 characters and the text is taken in one call (ARCHITECTURE.md 7.11). A recovered document takes over its old copy's id.

\*\* 715 tests. Search as M2.3 built it (ARCHITECTURE.md 7.1 and 7.10): the Find, Replace and Go to Line windows, the Search menu with F3 and Shift+F3, the always-on current-line band, and Replace All as a sliced job (STEPS 13a to 13f) that keeps the window alive, can be cancelled, and is one step of Undo. Adam's hands-on list on the 50 MB file passed. Measured: 289,088 matches replaced in 64.5 s (about 4,500 a second, flat); Undo of a 19,040-match run took 10.6 s with the window frozen; an open copy of the 50 MB file costs about 0.9 GB. Adam accepted the speed and decided only to document the cost of a big Undo (HANDOFF.md decisions; the options not taken are recorded there). A STEP 13f fix: while a job runs the page swallows Ctrl+Z, Ctrl+Y and Ctrl+Shift+Z, because a greyed Undo action does not consume its shortcut and the view's own binding ran. Known limit: highlighting every match on one huge line can freeze the window; it does not come from the job. The manual gained the topic "Find, Replace and Go to Line".

Decision attributions such as "(Adam, 2026-11-14)" were removed from both files. Each changed line is kept below as it was. The decisions are in the HANDOFF.md decision log and the archive.

### Line 1 (roadmap.md)

```
**Line rule** (Adam, 2026-11-18): no line of this file has more than 60 words. A gate's table row stays short. Longer detail and the notes go in `docs/gate-details.md`; a row that says "see gate-details" has more there. Check: `awk 'NF > 60' docs/roadmap.md` prints nothing.
```

### Line 2 (roadmap.md)

```
- **The Done column** (Adam, 2026-11-10). ☐ means not done. ☑ means done, followed by the date (YYYY/MM/DD). A note mark (\*, †, ‡, ¶, §, ‖, \#, then doubled: \*\*, ††, ...) points to the notes of that milestone in `docs/gate-details.md`; the marks start again in each milestone. Each milestone also has a Status line.
```

### Line 3 (roadmap.md)

```
- **Standing objective: REUSE certification** (Adam, 2026-11-17). The repository is to pass `reuse lint` (https://reuse.software), with the licence texts in `LICENSES/`. Everything created from 2026-11-17 on is compliant when it is made (AGENTS.md rule 5); older files are brought into line at M9. Already done: the vendored xxHash (`third_party/xxhash/`, with a `.license` sidecar) and `LICENSES/`.
```

- Audit of 10c2: HEAD `f139f98`; the four sha prefixes match on the device; evidence fresh (19:31 UTC build and ctest, 19:32 proof), 970 of 970, 0 warnings, the three new `UiResources` tests ran (911 to 913); the split `check_question.cpp` compiled first time, same 40 GTK names, largest function 30 lines; `reload_from_disk()` is the dictated 6 lines, enabled with `reopen-encoding`'s rule, Ctrl+R and the File menu order as specified; `main_window.cpp` +12 lines (365). The one Prompt doubt (the over-50 awk printed `main_window.cpp: 403: 91`) is my slip (`NR` instead of `FNR`); the 91-line function is the constructor, pre-existing and now noted as debt. **Verdict PASS.** **Next: Adam's hands-on of step 10; then step 11.**

### 2026-11-19 - M2.5 design talk, decisions in full (Adam; the plan is `claude/M2.5_plan.md`, section 1)
- Topic 1 (what the window holds): tabs Appearance, Editor, Files (Adam: consistent as ctxmon, the agent and other tools expand). Colours: Option A, no scheme choice, the editor colours follow the colour mode (Vermilion Classic light, a dark twin dark); the presets asked for at the M2.3 talk are dropped for now. Zoom In, Zoom Out, Reset Zoom and the Colour Mode menu item are parked. The Tab key always inserts spaces; the width is 4 or 8 and sets both the insertion and how a tab character is drawn.
- Controls rule (Adam, 2026-11-19; "no myriad of different options"): check box for on or off, drop-down for one of a fixed set, number box for a free number, font button for a font, text box for free text. No radio buttons (Adam abandoned them), no switches (they do not fit the Windows 2000 look).
- Topic 2: the dark palette is in the same gate (M2.5), before the window.
- Topic 3: everything takes effect at Apply with no restart, **debounced 100 ms** (Adam), no preview before Apply; the two programs read `settings.ini` at start only.
- Topic 4: a modal `GtkWindow` in the shared base; a Tools menu with Settings... in both programs (Option 1); shortcut **Ctrl+Comma** (Adam asked for one).
- New gate (Adam asked, 2026-11-19, after `./vermilion file` printed "This application can not open files"): **M2.4b, files from the command line**, before M2.5 (Adam agreed to the order and the name). Plan `claude/M2.4b_plan.md`. M2 has 16 gates; progress 21% (5 + 6 + 19 × 9/16 = 21.7).

## Part 36 — rotated out of HANDOFF.md on 2026-11-19 (fifteenth chat, plans accepted)

### 2026-11-18 (14:00, fifteenth chat) - M2.4 step 10 hands-on passed; step 11 docs sweep written
- Adam: "All steps passed" for the step 10 list. Wrote the sweep from the code (`external_messages.cpp`, `decide()`, `on_banner_*`) so the manual says what the program does: banner texts and buttons, "Reload Anyway" for a reloaded file that looks binary, a deleted file marked `*` with its recovery copy, Keep mine and Ignore moving the baseline, Ctrl+R with its questions, the setting's path. The REUSE header of the new topic is a sidecar because the manual parser treats an HTML comment as a fallback (checked in `manual_spans.cpp`). Process fact: `device_commit_files` wrote the previous content when I overwrote a file at an output path used before; every commit now uses a new path, and the result is checked by size and sha on the device. Also this chat: 10c2 audited PASS (970 of 970).

### Decisions of 2026-11-19, 11:52 to 12:05 (Adam's answers to the plan questions)
- **M2.4b:** a missing file is asked about on the terminal, `(Y/n)`, and on yes created on open (the file is created when answered, so no "no file yet" state in the baseline). A folder is refused with "XXX is a directory, not a file"; opening every document of a folder with a Y/n question is a future idea. `file:line` is left out. Consequence: `G_APPLICATION_HANDLES_COMMAND_LINE` replaces HANDLE_OPEN (the `open` signal runs in the primary instance and cannot reach a second launch's terminal); with no file arguments the handler calls activate; without a terminal nothing is asked or created and the status bar says the file does not exist. The gate has four steps (core, command-line handler, queue and window, docs and hands-on).
- **M2.5:** settings keys use underscores (`colour_mode`, `highlight_current_line`, `tab_width`, `long_line_limit`, and the existing key renamed to `confirm_large_reload` in step 1; nothing was released, the old spelling is not read). Follow system through the portal: build it, test it later. The "Use the system monospace font" check box: accepted. Light and Dark can be chosen by hand at any time, Follow system is the third entry. Dark palette from Greybird-dark's colours (window `#3b3e3f`, base `#2d2e30`, text `#eeeeec`, selection `#145ba6`, borders `#1d1f1f`, disabled `#959696` and `#353839`); table in the M2.5 plan section 6.
- Adam granted read access to `/usr/share/themes/Greybird-dark/gtk-3.0` for this chat; `device_stage_files` read `gtk.css` (314 KB).
